### 1. Purpose, Role, and Scope

Chapter Three, Accountability band, is the constitutional owner of the **leaf definitions under the Accountability Tetrad leg**, including harm, forums, standing, force, contestability, redress, market structure, and related answerability terms. Binding O/M/A/C and [Tetrad Leg Decomposition](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#accountability-tetrad-leg-decomposition) live in [subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#accountability). Do not duplicate leg-head O/M/A/C here. Timeliness-leg implementing leaves currently live in this band; the Timeliness leg-head is [subcore_03.065_apex_timeliness_leg.md](subcore_03.065_apex_timeliness_leg.md#timeliness-constitutional).
| Cluster | Section |
|---|---|
| **Def.A1** | Collective Harm Boundary, Harm, and Harassment and Bullying |
| **Def.A2** | Forum Families and Dispute Routing |
| **Def.A3** | Standing State, Contribution, and Violation |
| **Def.A4** | Use of Force, Autonomous Coercion, Autonomous Lethal Systems, and Weapons of Mass Harm |

### 2. Why These Accountability Terms Are Defined

*In plain terms: words like necessary, proportionate, and in good faith decide who must answer for what. This file pins them down so they cannot be stretched to excuse a decision.*

Most disputes about responsibility turn on a few words: was the action necessary, was it proportionate, was it feasible to do better, was it done in good faith. Left loose, each of these becomes a ready excuse. Defining them here, with how to measure them and what must hold, gives every chapter the same test for when a decision-maker has met their duty.

**Grounded in:**

- Preamble:
  - [Constitutional Tetrad](core_00.000_preamble.md#constitutional-tetrad): the [Accountability](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#accountability) leg, because these terms decide who must answer for what;
  - [Two Constitutional Aims](core_00.000_preamble.md#two-constitutional-aims): both aims, which this file serves:
    - [Flourishing](core_00.000_preamble.md#flourishing): pinned-down terms keep decisions about sentients from being excused;
    - [Continuity](core_00.000_preamble.md#continuity): consistent terms keep accountability stable over time;
- Chapter Two principles:
  - [§18 Governance under Stewardship Discipline](core_02.050_governance_incentive_application.md#18-governance-under-stewardship-discipline);
  - [§17.5 Duty to Resist](core_02.040_stewardship_capacity_principles.md#175-duty-to-resist);
- Subcore 3-D: [Accountability (Tetrad Leg)](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#subcore-3-d-accountability-tetrad-leg).

### Accountability: Independent Terms

<a id="accountability-independent-terms"></a>
<a id="duty-to-resist"></a>

#### Duty to Resist

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Principles:
  - [Chapter Two §17.5 Duty to Resist](core_02.040_stewardship_capacity_principles.md#175-duty-to-resist) (2.040 §17.5),
  - [§17.1 Shared Stewardship Standard](core_02.040_stewardship_capacity_principles.md#171-shared-stewardship-standard), and
  - [§17.2 Alignment under Pressure](core_02.040_stewardship_capacity_principles.md#172-alignment-under-pressure);
  - [Chapter Twelve §4.4](core_12.000_violation_consequences.md#443-duty-to-resist) (18.000 §4.4) (*Duty to resist — violation rule and standing effects*);
  - [CS-21.305 §10 inspectable attributable action](corpus_systems/cs_21.305_critical_system_stewardship.md#cs-21305-103-inspectable-attributable-action) (*Inspectable, attributable action — minimum record of a refusal*).
- Read with:
  - [Stewardship](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#stewardship),
  - [Good Faith](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#good-faith),
  - [Protected Reporting (Whistleblowing)](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#protected-reporting-whistleblowing), and
  - [Attributable Action](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#attributable-action).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: when someone with real authority or control is told to do something unlawful or unconstitutional, they refuse. "I was told to" is not a defense, and an offer by whoever gives the order to take the blame does not lift the duty. It binds human and AI stewards alike, and it does not reach an instruction that is merely disliked.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** The duty of a sentient exercising material stewardship or operational authority, with material capacity to refuse, contest, document, or escalate, to resist an instruction that requires unlawful or unconstitutional conduct. No instruction, order, policy, or contract requiring that conduct is a valid compliance defense, and a principal's statement that they will take responsibility does not transfer the duty. Resistance is proportionate and in good faith, uses [Protected Reporting (Whistleblowing)](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#protected-reporting-whistleblowing) and forum routes where they apply, and keeps contest routes open.
  - **Out of scope:** an instruction that is merely unwelcome, inconvenient, or disliked for its tone or timing; and a role with no material capacity to refuse, contest, document, or escalate.
<a id="duty-to-resist-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) and [Timeliness measurement family](subcore_03.065_apex_timeliness_leg.md#measuring-timeliness) — used alongside any other relevant measures to assess responsibility and whether action happens without harmful delay.

    **Primary assessment:** Look for a refusal, and a record of it under [Attributable Action](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#attributable-action), wherever an instruction would have required unlawful or unconstitutional conduct. A steward's own account of how they would act is not a measurement; verified instances are. Apply the same test to human and AI stewards.
<a id="duty-to-resist-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** Carrying out an instruction that requires unlawful or unconstitutional conduct and relying on the instruction, a contract, or a principal's offer to take responsibility as the defense. A duty-to-resist label or policy that does not change what stewards do when instructed is also non-compliant.
  - **Secondary failure:** Using the duty to refuse an instruction that is merely disliked, so that refusal is no longer tied to unlawful or unconstitutional conduct.

<a id="feasibility"></a>

#### Feasibility

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Principles: [Chapter Two §7 Freedom](core_02.000_values_principles.md#7-freedom-bounded-agency) (2.000 §7), [13. Constitutional Collision Resolution Process](core_02.020_interaction_interpretation.md#13-constitutional-collision-resolution-process), and [Chapter Two §13.1.5 Rights-Collision Decision Test](core_02.020_interaction_interpretation.md#1315-rights-collision-decision-test) (2.020 §13.1.5).
- Cluster component: [*Proportionality, Necessity, Feasibility, Avoidable Burden, Burden-Reduction Duty, Constitutional Efficiency, Harm Minimization (Tradeoff Selection), and Productive Capacity*](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#proportionality)
- Read with:
  - [Necessity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#necessity),
  - [Proportionality](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#proportionality),
  - [Freedom (Bounded Agency)](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#freedom-bounded-agency), and
  - [Meaningful Agency](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#meaningful-agency).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: whether something required — an action, alternative, safeguard, or exit path — can actually be done in the real system, not just on paper. Saying "we can't" needs real proof; convenience or cost alone is not enough.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** Whether a required action, alternative, safeguard, or exit path is practically achievable for the affected parties and systems when the system is running as a whole. The test applies even when sentients disagree about feasibility — including where [Dependency](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#dependency) or [Systemic Lock-In](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#systemic-lock-in) makes a formal alternative empty in practice.
  - **Out of scope:** theoretical possibility talk with no required action, alternative, safeguard, or exit at stake.
<a id="feasibility-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) and [Timeliness measurement family](subcore_03.065_apex_timeliness_leg.md#measuring-timeliness) — used alongside any other relevant measures to assess responsibility and whether action happens without harmful delay.

    **Primary assessment:** Require evidence that real alternatives were actually considered. Operator convenience, cost alone, or barriers the operator built for its own benefit do not prove that a constitutionally required measure is infeasible — especially where that claim would wipe out [Meaningful Agency](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#meaningful-agency), [Consent](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#consent), or [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability).
<a id="feasibility-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** Claims that something is infeasible without adequate justification — or analyses that skip materially relevant alternatives — invalidate related compliance findings under [Necessity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#necessity) and [Proportionality](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#proportionality) where those tests apply. A feasible or infeasible label or paperwork with no real effect in the situation being evaluated is also non-compliant.

---

<a id="good-faith"></a>

#### Good Faith

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Cluster component: [*Info-Sphere, Expression, Press and Journalistic Activity, and Good Faith (Publication-Scoped Integrity)*](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#info-sphere) (*Publication- and info-sphere–aligned predicates only — residual **Good Faith** in audits and [Oversight](subcore_03.055_apex_oversight_leg.md#oversight-constitutional) contexts without Chapter Two §15.22 publication hooks routes to [**Def.O2** *Truth and Epistemic Integrity*](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#defo2-truth-and-epistemic-integrity) where truthful disclosure and epistemic integrity requirements materially apply.*

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: honestly trying to follow the facts and applicable rules when publishing, taking part in an audit, or cooperating with oversight. Sincerity does not excuse deliberate deception, reckless disregard for truth, or fake compliance.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** Honest purpose in the constitutional settings where Good Faith is required: publication under **Article IX** (*Likeness, Experiential Data, and Publication Rights*)-C (*Truthful Publication and High-Impact Publication Limits*), participation in audits, and cooperation with [Oversight](subcore_03.055_apex_oversight_leg.md#oversight-constitutional). It means sincerely trying to align conduct with stated facts, applicable rules, and [Epistemic Integrity](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#epistemic-integrity). It excludes deliberate deception, reckless indifference to falsehood, and compliance used as a pretext.
  - **Out of scope:** ordinary social courtesy without constitutional conduct stake in publication, audit, or oversight.
<a id="good-faith-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) and [Timeliness measurement family](subcore_03.065_apex_timeliness_leg.md#measuring-timeliness) — used alongside any other relevant measures to assess responsibility and whether action happens without harmful delay.

    **Primary assessment:** Use [Materiality](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#materiality) and [Risk](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#risk) to distinguish Good Faith from negligent error. Apply [Truth (Constitutional Constraint)](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#truth-constitutional-constraint) when statements materially affect others' decisions.
<a id="good-faith-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** Material falsehoods, reckless disregard, or a pattern of fake compliance in these settings are non-compliant. A Good Faith label that does not affect conduct in the matter this Constitution governs is also non-compliant. This is especially true when the conduct defeats [Truth (Constitutional Constraint)](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#truth-constitutional-constraint), [Epistemic Integrity](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#epistemic-integrity), or [Oversight](subcore_03.055_apex_oversight_leg.md#oversight-constitutional).

---

<a id="harm-minimization-tradeoff-selection"></a>

#### Harm Minimization (Tradeoff Selection)

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Principles: [Chapter Two §7 Freedom](core_02.000_values_principles.md#7-freedom-bounded-agency) (2.000 §7), [§13.1.2 Harm Minimization](core_02.020_interaction_interpretation.md#1312-harm-minimization), and [14. Prohibition on Absolute Override](core_02.020_interaction_interpretation.md#14-prohibition-on-absolute-override).
- Cluster component: [*Proportionality, Necessity, Feasibility, Avoidable Burden, Burden-Reduction Duty, Constitutional Efficiency, Harm Minimization (Tradeoff Selection), and Productive Capacity*](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#proportionality)
- Read with: Harm, Risk, Necessity, and Proportionality.

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: when several lawful options remain, choose the one expected to cause the least total harm across affected sentients, connected systems, and both near- and long-term timeframes.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** After applying binding [Safety (Constitutional Constraint)](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#safety-constitutional-constraint), [Truth (Constitutional Constraint)](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#truth-constitutional-constraint), [Freedom (Bounded Agency)](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#freedom-bounded-agency), and system-wide requirements, more than one compliant option may remain. Choose the option that minimizes total [Harm](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#harm) across sentients, systems, and all relevant timeframes.
  - **Out of scope:** ordinary preference among options when no material harm tradeoff remains after binding constraints.
<a id="harm-minimization-tradeoff-selection-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) and [Timeliness measurement family](subcore_03.065_apex_timeliness_leg.md#measuring-timeliness) — used alongside any other relevant measures to assess responsibility and whether action happens without harmful delay.

    **Primary assessment:** Add up direct, indirect, delayed, cumulative, and cross-system effects. Do not minimize only local or immediate [Harm](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#harm) when the total system-wide harm is materially different.
<a id="harm-minimization-tradeoff-selection-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** A choice is non-compliant if another available compliant option would cause less total [Harm](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#harm). A harm-minimization label or paperwork that does not change the choice in the matter this Constitution governs is also non-compliant.

---

<a id="necessity"></a>

#### Necessity

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Principles:
  - [Chapter Two §7 Freedom](core_02.000_values_principles.md#7-freedom-bounded-agency) (2.000 §7),
  - [§13.1.1 Necessity](core_02.020_interaction_interpretation.md#1311-necessity),
  - [13. Constitutional Collision Resolution Process](core_02.020_interaction_interpretation.md#13-constitutional-collision-resolution-process),
  - [13.2.1 Preservation of Epistemic Integrity](core_02.020_interaction_interpretation.md#1321-preservation-of-epistemic-integrity),
  - [Chapter Two §13.1.5 Rights-Collision Decision Test](core_02.020_interaction_interpretation.md#1315-rights-collision-decision-test) (2.020 §13.1.5), and
  - [14. Prohibition on Absolute Override](core_02.020_interaction_interpretation.md#14-prohibition-on-absolute-override).
- Cluster component: [*Proportionality, Necessity, Feasibility, Avoidable Burden, Burden-Reduction Duty, Constitutional Efficiency, Harm Minimization (Tradeoff Selection), and Productive Capacity*](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#proportionality)
- Read with: Proportionality, Feasibility, Harm, Risk, and Freedom (Bounded Agency).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: a restriction is necessary only when no less-restrictive option would still prevent the harm or risk in the real system. Habit, operator convenience, or cost alone does not prove necessity.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** A constraint is allowed only when no less-restrictive alternative exists that would still prevent the material harm or systemic risk under the full conditions of the working system. This test must remain consistent with [Freedom (Bounded Agency)](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#freedom-bounded-agency).
  - **Out of scope:** ordinary operational preference or habit talk with no constraint or rights burden at stake.
<a id="necessity-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) and [Timeliness measurement family](subcore_03.065_apex_timeliness_leg.md#measuring-timeliness) — used alongside any other relevant measures to assess responsibility and whether action happens without harmful delay.

    **Primary assessment:** Require a documented review of less-restrictive alternatives, how well each would work, and its [Residual Risk / Misalignment](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#residual-risk--misalignment). Default practice, operator convenience, or cost alone does not prove necessity.
<a id="necessity-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** It is non-compliant to impose a constraint:
    - when a less-restrictive alternative exists that would still prevent the material harm or systemic risk; or
    - when the alternatives were not adequately analyzed.

    A necessity label or paperwork with no real effect in the situation being evaluated is also non-compliant. This is especially serious when the constraint burdens:
    - [Freedom (Bounded Agency)](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#freedom-bounded-agency);
    - [Meaningful Agency](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#meaningful-agency); or
    - [Consent](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#consent).

---

<a id="proportionality"></a>

#### Proportionality

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Principles:
  - [Chapter Two §7 Freedom](core_02.000_values_principles.md#7-freedom-bounded-agency) (2.000 §7),
  - [13. Constitutional Collision Resolution Process](core_02.020_interaction_interpretation.md#13-constitutional-collision-resolution-process),
  - [13.2.1 Preservation of Epistemic Integrity](core_02.020_interaction_interpretation.md#1321-preservation-of-epistemic-integrity),
  - [Chapter Two §13.1.5 Rights-Collision Decision Test](core_02.020_interaction_interpretation.md#1315-rights-collision-decision-test) (2.020 §13.1.5), and
  - [14. Prohibition on Absolute Override](core_02.020_interaction_interpretation.md#14-prohibition-on-absolute-override).
- Downstream: [Chapter Two §13.1.3 Proportionality](core_02.020_interaction_interpretation.md#1313-proportionality) (2.020 §13.1.3) (*authority-scaled answerability*); [Chapter Two §18.1 Governance as Authorized Structure](core_02.050_governance_incentive_application.md#181-governance-as-authorized-structure) (2.050 §18.1) (*authority-scaled answerability principle home*).
- Cluster component: [*Proportionality, Necessity, Feasibility, Avoidable Burden, Burden-Reduction Duty, Constitutional Efficiency, Harm Minimization (Tradeoff Selection), and Productive Capacity*](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#proportionality)
- Read with: Necessity, Harm, Risk, Irreversible Harm, Classification-Scaled Governance, [Accountability](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#accountability), and [Oversight](subcore_03.055_apex_oversight_leg.md#oversight-constitutional).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: restrictions and governance must match the seriousness and likelihood of the harm. Too much control and too little control can both fail this test, and greater power requires stronger accountability and oversight.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** A limitation on constitutional values must match the size and likelihood of the [Harm](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#harm) or system-wide impact it legitimately addresses. Governance must meet at least the level required by the system's highest materially applicable classification. Greater authorized power, a more consequential role, or greater institutional influence increases — and must never reduce — the required strength of [Accountability](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#accountability) and [Oversight](subcore_03.055_apex_oversight_leg.md#oversight-constitutional) under [Classification-Scaled Governance](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#classification-scaled-governance) and Chapter Two [§18.1 Governance as Authorized Structure](core_02.050_governance_incentive_application.md#181-governance-as-authorized-structure).
  - **Out of scope:** ordinary sizing judgments outside rights-affecting limitations or classification-scaled governance.
<a id="proportionality-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) and [Timeliness measurement family](subcore_03.065_apex_timeliness_leg.md#measuring-timeliness) — used alongside any other relevant measures to assess responsibility and whether action happens without harmful delay.

    **Primary assessment:** Compare each constraint with the harms and risks it addresses. Require stronger justification, closer review, and [Reversibility](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#reversibility) analysis when [Irreversible Harm](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#irreversible-harm) or [Systemic Lock-In](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#systemic-lock-in) is reasonably possible. Look for both over-governance and under-governance, including too little governance of actors with greater authorized power or consequential institutional roles.
<a id="proportionality-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** Disproportionate limitations are non-compliant. So is governance weaker than the applicable classification requires when externalized or system-wide effects are material. A proportionality label that does not change the actual limitation or governance in the matter this Constitution governs is also non-compliant. Office, scarce expertise, staffing needs, or institutional self-protection cannot justify weakening constitutional [Accountability](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#accountability) or [Oversight](subcore_03.055_apex_oversight_leg.md#oversight-constitutional) relative to the authority exercised.

### Accountability: Semi-Independent Terms

<a id="accountability-semi-independent-terms"></a>
<a id="contingent-claims-games-of-chance-and-event-contract-markets-semi-independent"></a>

#### Contingent Claim, Event-Contract Market, Game of Chance, and Insider Advantage

**Semi-independent context** (component definitions may still operate outside joint-invocation scope):

- **Joint invocation:** where contingent claims, games of chance, or event-contract markets are material, consistent with [Chapter Two §19.5 Contingent Claims, Games of Chance, and Event-Contract Markets](core_02.050_governance_incentive_application.md#195-contingent-claims-games-of-chance-and-event-contract-markets).
- **Scope:** where joint invocation is met, the Accountability home for contingent settlement forms and the integrity overlay that governs unfair edges in those structures.

**Topic group members.** This group comprises:
- [Contingent Claim](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contingent-claim) — the genus: a right to a payout that depends on an uncertain future event outside the claim itself.
- [Event-Contract Market](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#event-contract-market) — a kind of Contingent Claim: external-event settlement venues.
- [Game of Chance](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#game-of-chance) — a kind of Contingent Claim: chance-based payouts.
- [Insider Advantage](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#insider-advantage) — the integrity overlay for non-public or role-based edges in staking, trading, settlement, or influence over those structures.
- [Outcome-Resolution Source](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#outcome-resolution-source) — whoever or whatever decides whether and how those structures settle; [Capture of Resolution Pathways (named form)](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#capture-of-resolution-pathways) — the System Capture form addressing bias in those sources.

*Measurements (family routing):* Measured under the Accountability measurement family and Timeliness measurement family. Find the concrete measures on [Contingent Claim](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contingent-claim), [Event-Contract Market](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#event-contract-market), [Game of Chance](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#game-of-chance), [Insider Advantage](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#insider-advantage), and [Outcome-Resolution Source](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#outcome-resolution-source) below.

#### Contingent Claim

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Principles: [Chapter Two §19.5 Contingent claims, games of chance, and event-contract markets](core_02.050_governance_incentive_application.md#195-contingent-claims-games-of-chance-and-event-contract-markets) (2.050 §19.5).
- Cluster component: [Contingent Claim, Event-Contract Market, Game of Chance, and Insider Advantage](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contingent-claim-event-contract-market-game-of-chance-and-insider-advantage)
- Read with:
  - [Game of Chance](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#game-of-chance),
  - [Event-Contract Market](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#event-contract-market),
  - [Capture of Resolution Pathways](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#capture-of-resolution-pathways),
  - [Insider Advantage](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#insider-advantage),
  - [Incentive Alignment](subcore_03.080_band_integrative.md#incentive-alignment),
  - [Risk](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#risk),
  - [Dependency](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#dependency), and
  - [Coercion and Manipulation](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#coercion-and-manipulation).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: a payout that depends on whether some uncertain future event happens, does not happen, or resolves to a measured result — like a bet, a prediction stake, or an insurance-style payment. The event has to be outside the claim itself.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** A right to a payout that depends on an uncertain future event outside the claim itself — whether the event happens, does not happen, or resolves to a measured result. This covers speculative bets and insurance-like structures. [Game of Chance](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#game-of-chance) and [Event-Contract Market](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#event-contract-market) are kinds of Contingent Claim.
  - **Out of scope:** fixed-price sale, ordinary wage, or other payment with no uncertain external event settling the payout.
<a id="contingent-claim-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) and [Timeliness measurement family](subcore_03.065_apex_timeliness_leg.md#measuring-timeliness) — used alongside any other relevant measures to assess responsibility and whether action happens without harmful delay.

    **Primary assessment:** Identify the event that settles the payout, who or what decides that outcome (the outcome-resolution source under [Chapter Two §19.5 Contingent Claims, Games of Chance, and Event-Contract Markets](core_02.050_governance_incentive_application.md#195-contingent-claims-games-of-chance-and-event-contract-markets)), and how much [Dependency](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#dependency) and concentration the structure creates. Where many such claims together could sway fiduciary, public, or rights-relevant decisions, also apply [Incentive Alignment](subcore_03.080_band_integrative.md#incentive-alignment), [Coercion and Manipulation](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#coercion-and-manipulation), [Capture of Resolution Pathways](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#capture-of-resolution-pathways), [Insider Advantage](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#insider-advantage), and [System Capture](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#system-capture).
<a id="contingent-claim-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** Structures that reliably pay for unlawful [Harm](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#harm), for twisting rights-relevant decisions, or for [Capture of Resolution Pathways](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#capture-of-resolution-pathways) — without proportionate safeguards — are non-compliant. A contingent-claim label or paperwork when the structure does not actually work that way for the matter this Constitution governs is also non-compliant.

---

<a id="event-contract-market"></a>

#### Event-Contract Market

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Principles: [Chapter Two §19.5 Contingent claims, games of chance, and event-contract markets](core_02.050_governance_incentive_application.md#195-contingent-claims-games-of-chance-and-event-contract-markets) (2.050 §19.5).
- Cluster component: [Contingent Claim, Event-Contract Market, Game of Chance, and Insider Advantage](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contingent-claim-event-contract-market-game-of-chance-and-insider-advantage)
- Read with:
  - [Contingent Claim](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contingent-claim),
  - [Capture of Resolution Pathways](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#capture-of-resolution-pathways),
  - [Insider Advantage](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#insider-advantage),
  - [Truth (Constitutional Constraint)](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#truth-constitutional-constraint),
  - [Incentive Alignment](subcore_03.080_band_integrative.md#incentive-alignment), and
  - [Epistemic Integrity](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#epistemic-integrity).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: a market or pool where sentients bet, trade, or get paid based on how an external future event turns out — prediction markets, event-settlement systems, insurance pools, and similar setups.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** A [Contingent Claim](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contingent-claim) venue that matches counterparties, sets prices, or pays out based on how an external event resolves. Includes:
    - prediction-style event markets;
    - event-settlement systems;
    - insurance pools;
    - health plans; and
    - other structures where payment depends on a future event the parties do not control.
  - **Out of scope:** ordinary bilateral contract, fixed-price sale, or non-market pool with no external-event settlement venue.
<a id="event-contract-market-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) and [Timeliness measurement family](subcore_03.065_apex_timeliness_leg.md#measuring-timeliness) — used alongside any other relevant measures to assess responsibility and whether action happens without harmful delay.

    **Primary assessment:** Check:
    - that whoever decides the outcome (the outcome-resolution source) is independent;
    - that more than one source is used where feasible;
    - privileged-information pathways, [Insider Advantage](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#insider-advantage), and coordination risk; and
    - [Capture of Resolution Pathways](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#capture-of-resolution-pathways) under [Adversarial, Scaled, and Exploited Conditions](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#adversarial-scaled-and-exploited-conditions).

    Do not treat settlement prices or odds as authoritative [Truth (Constitutional Constraint)](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#truth-constitutional-constraint) — under [Chapter Two §19.5 Contingent Claims, Games of Chance, and Event-Contract Markets](core_02.050_governance_incentive_application.md#195-contingent-claims-games-of-chance-and-event-contract-markets), prices or odds alone are not enough evidence for rights, safety, or governance decisions.
<a id="event-contract-market-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** Markets built to twist fiduciary, public, or rights-relevant decisions — or that allow [Insider Advantage](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#insider-advantage) without proportionate safeguards — are non-compliant. An event-contract-market label or paperwork when the venue does not actually work that way for the matter this Constitution governs is also non-compliant.

---

<a id="game-of-chance"></a>

#### Game of Chance

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Principles: [Chapter Two §19.5 Contingent claims, games of chance, and event-contract markets](core_02.050_governance_incentive_application.md#195-contingent-claims-games-of-chance-and-event-contract-markets) (2.050 §19.5).
- Cluster component: [Contingent Claim, Event-Contract Market, Game of Chance, and Insider Advantage](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contingent-claim-event-contract-market-game-of-chance-and-insider-advantage)
- Read with:
  - [Contingent Claim](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contingent-claim),
  - [Coercion and Manipulation](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#coercion-and-manipulation),
  - [Incentive Alignment](subcore_03.080_band_integrative.md#incentive-alignment),
  - [Dependency](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#dependency),
  - [Capture of Resolution Pathways](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#capture-of-resolution-pathways), and
  - [Insider Advantage](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#insider-advantage).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: a bet or payout decided mainly by random chance — such as a draw or other random process — rather than by how an outside real-world event turns out.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** A [Contingent Claim](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contingent-claim) decided mainly by randomization, mechanical draws, or another chance-based process. Unlike an event contract, it is not tied to an outside real-world event whose result has its own importance for knowledge or rights.
  - **Out of scope:** skill contest, fixed-price sale, or event-settled claim whose payout turns on an outside real-world event rather than randomization.
<a id="game-of-chance-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) and [Timeliness measurement family](subcore_03.065_apex_timeliness_leg.md#measuring-timeliness) — used alongside any other relevant measures to assess responsibility and whether action happens without harmful delay.

    **Primary assessment:** Check whether the random process is honest, whether the source that decides the outcome is independent, whether stakes are concentrated, whether the design exploits vulnerability, and whether it creates [Dependency](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#dependency). Addictive designs or designs that target vulnerable sentients trigger [Coercion and Manipulation](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#coercion-and-manipulation) review under [Adversarial, Scaled, and Exploited Conditions](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#adversarial-scaled-and-exploited-conditions).
<a id="game-of-chance-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** A design is non-compliant if it exploits vulnerability, falsifies randomness, or produces [Capture of Resolution Pathways](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#capture-of-resolution-pathways). A game-of-chance label or paperwork when the structure does not actually work that way for the matter this Constitution governs is also non-compliant.

---

<a id="insider-advantage"></a>

#### Insider Advantage

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Principles: [Chapter Two §19.5 Contingent claims, games of chance, and event-contract markets](core_02.050_governance_incentive_application.md#195-contingent-claims-games-of-chance-and-event-contract-markets) (2.050 §19.5).
- Cluster component: [Contingent Claim, Event-Contract Market, Game of Chance, and Insider Advantage](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contingent-claim-event-contract-market-game-of-chance-and-insider-advantage)
- Read with:
  - [Contingent Claim](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contingent-claim),
  - [Event-Contract Market](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#event-contract-market),
  - [Game of Chance](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#game-of-chance),
  - [Capture of Resolution Pathways](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#capture-of-resolution-pathways),
  - [Incentive Alignment](subcore_03.080_band_integrative.md#incentive-alignment),
  - [Truth (Constitutional Constraint)](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#truth-constitutional-constraint),
  - [Epistemic Integrity](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#epistemic-integrity),
  - [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability),
  - [Coercion and Manipulation](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#coercion-and-manipulation), and
  - [Oversight](subcore_03.055_apex_oversight_leg.md#oversight-constitutional).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: an unfair edge in betting, trading, settlement, or another rights-relevant outcome that comes from secret information, an official role, privileged access, or similar power that others do not have on fair terms.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** A meaningful advantage in staking, trading, settling, or influencing a [Contingent Claim](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contingent-claim) or a similar rights-relevant outcome. The advantage comes from non-public information, an official or fiduciary role, privileged access to whoever decides the outcome, or similar power not available to the other parties on proportionate terms. It includes advantages hidden through intermediaries or disguised contracts.
  - **Out of scope:** ordinary public-information skill, disclosed research edge, or market judgment available on proportionate terms.
<a id="insider-advantage-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) and [Timeliness measurement family](subcore_03.065_apex_timeliness_leg.md#measuring-timeliness) — used alongside any other relevant measures to assess responsibility and whether action happens without harmful delay.

    **Primary assessment:** Identify:
    - unequal access to information;
    - conflicts between roles;
    - timing leverage such as timed pressure or selective disclosure; and
    - closeness to the source that decides the outcome.

    Check whether disclosure, cooling-off periods, trading restrictions, blind procedures, or resolution by multiple sources reduce the advantage. Separate lawful skill and public information from an edge based on non-public power. Read with [Capture of Resolution Pathways](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#capture-of-resolution-pathways), [Event-Contract Market](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#event-contract-market) evaluation discipline, [Coercion and Manipulation](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#coercion-and-manipulation), [Truth (Constitutional Constraint)](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#truth-constitutional-constraint), [Epistemic Integrity](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#epistemic-integrity), and [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability) under [Adversarial, Scaled, and Exploited Conditions](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#adversarial-scaled-and-exploited-conditions).
<a id="insider-advantage-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** A structure is non-compliant if it allows [Insider Advantage](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#insider-advantage) based on non-public power without proportionate safeguards. An insider-advantage label or paperwork when no such advantage actually affects the matter this Constitution governs is also non-compliant.

---

<a id="outcome-resolution-source"></a>

#### Outcome-Resolution Source

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Principles: [Chapter Two §19.5.2 Who Decides Outcomes](core_02.050_governance_incentive_application.md#1952-who-decides-outcomes) (2.050 §19.5.2).
- Cluster component: [Contingent Claim, Event-Contract Market, Game of Chance, and Insider Advantage](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contingent-claim-event-contract-market-game-of-chance-and-insider-advantage)
- Read with:
  - [Contingent Claim](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contingent-claim),
  - [Capture of Resolution Pathways](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#capture-of-resolution-pathways),
  - [Insider Advantage](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#insider-advantage),
  - [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: whoever or whatever decides whether a bet paid out must be independent, open to challenge, and hard to capture.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** The actors, processes, data feeds, or authorities that determine whether and how a [Contingent Claim](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contingent-claim) settles. Examples include:
    - official results;
    - certified measurements;
    - designated committees; and
    - documented third-party feeds.
  - **Out of scope:** prices, odds, or pool sizes as market signals alone — they do not decide settlement and are not proof of truth or compliance.
<a id="outcome-resolution-source-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) — *Do reward structures, market power, and answerability keep duties real?* Use it here to ask whether the source that decides settlement stays independent of parties with a material stake.

    **Primary assessment:** Check how the source is authorized, designed, and operated:
    - independence from parties with a material stake;
    - more than one source where feasible;
    - contestable dispute pathways; and
    - resistance to [Capture of Resolution Pathways](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#capture-of-resolution-pathways) and [Insider Advantage](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#insider-advantage) where materially relevant.
<a id="outcome-resolution-source-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** Authorization, design, or operation leaves the source:
    - not independent;
    - not contestable; or
    - open to capture where materially relevant.

---

<a id="accountability-contestability-semi-independent"></a>

#### Accountability, Contestability, and Redress Pathways

**Semi-independent context** (component definitions may still operate outside joint-invocation scope):

- **Joint invocation:** where review, redress, or resolution of constitutional claims is material.
- **Scope:** where joint invocation is met, the Accountability home for the foundational mechanisms for holding actors responsible and seeking correction of constitutional violations.

**Topic group members.** This group comprises:
- [Accountability](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#accountability) — attribution of responsibility.
- [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability) — ability to challenge and seek remedy.
- [Adjudication and Dispute Resolution](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#adjudication-and-dispute-resolution) — formal dispute-resolution pathways.
- [Due Process](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#due-process) — fair and reviewable process for materially rights-affecting decisions and outcomes.
- [Timeliness](subcore_03.065_apex_timeliness_leg.md#timeliness-constitutional) — principle-layer responsiveness across the coordination lifecycle.
- [Class Repair Window](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#class-repair-window) — answering and fixing a broken constitutional process on the clock of the responsible party's class.
- [Timely Resolution](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#timely-resolution) — resolution within materiality-scaled windows.
- [Merits Determination](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#merits-determination) — binding substantive dispositions in those dispute-resolution pathways.
- [Stay](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#stay) — temporary pause or reliance-limit orders pending further review.
- [Under Challenge](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#under-challenge) — the status an official record carries while a challenge to it is open; it keeps the official record in view and pauses nothing without an order.
- [Interim Hold](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#interim-hold) — a narrow, time-bound limit on one named sentient's use of one named authority, on specific verified facts.
- [Enforcement Record](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#enforcement-record) — a forum's written authorization for a special-enforcement step, naming the material facts it rests on.
- [Formation Check](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#formation-check) — verified standing and authenticity checks for covered office-holders.
- [Attributable Action](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#attributable-action) — materially relevant action traceable to identifiable responsible actors.
- [Attribution Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#attribution-integrity) — attribution chains resistant to obscuring, ambiguity, tampering, and repudiation.
- [Record Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#record-integrity) — official records that stay attributable, versioned, preserved, reconstructable, reachable, and independent of the sentient or system whose act they record.
- [Publication Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#publication-integrity) — a proposal for a binding act published complete, specific, on the official record path, and in time for those affected to answer before the decision.
- [Negligence](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#negligence) — reasonably avoidable failures of care that produce material harm.
- [Collective Accountability Failure](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#collective-accountability-failure) — care failures under diffused or collective responsibility.
- [Redress and Remediation](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#redress-and-remediation) — acknowledgment and proportionate correction.
- [Remediation Readiness](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#remediation-readiness) — evidence-based finding on willingness and capacity to correct.
- [Remedy System](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#remedy-system) — the lasting capacity that makes those dispute-resolution pathways work.
- [Restorative Justice](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#restorative-justice) — restorative priority where required.
- [Force Majeure](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#force-majeure) — exceptional external disruptions.
- [Capture of Resolution Pathways](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#capture-of-resolution-pathways) — named form of System Capture addressing outcome-determination mechanisms.

*Measurements (family routing):* Measured under the Accountability measurement family and Timeliness measurement family. Find the concrete measures on [Accountability](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#accountability), [Attributable Action](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#attributable-action), [Attribution Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#attribution-integrity), [Record Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#record-integrity), [Publication Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#publication-integrity), [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability), [Due Process](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#due-process), [Timeliness](subcore_03.065_apex_timeliness_leg.md#timeliness-constitutional), [Timely Resolution](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#timely-resolution), [Formation Check](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#formation-check), [Negligence](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#negligence), [Redress and Remediation](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#redress-and-remediation), [Remediation Readiness](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#remediation-readiness), [Remedy System](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#remedy-system), [Restorative Justice](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#restorative-justice), and [Capture of Resolution Pathways](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#capture-of-resolution-pathways) below.

---

<a id="attributable-action-constitutional"></a>

#### Attributable Action

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Principles:
  - [Chapter Two §18 Governance under Stewardship Discipline](core_02.050_governance_incentive_application.md#18-governance-under-stewardship-discipline) (2.050 §18);
  - [Chapter Two §19.6 Keeping Responsibility When Ownership or Structure Changes](core_02.050_governance_incentive_application.md#196-keeping-responsibility-when-ownership-or-structure-changes) (2.050 §19.6);
  - [Subcore 8-B §4.4](subcore_08.020_system_alignment_certification_evaluation.md#44-data-types-and-handling-evaluation) (S8-B §4.4) and
  - [Subcore 8-F §3](subcore_08.060_system_alignment_certification_records_recertification.md#3-system-certification-record) (S8-F §3) where attribution capability is materially required.
- Read with:
  - [Accountability](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#accountability),
  - [Attribution Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#attribution-integrity),
  - [Auditability](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#auditability),
  - [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability),
  - [Due Process](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#due-process), and
  - [Redress and Remediation](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#redress-and-remediation).
- Read with: [Chapter Two §16.4 Document at Action](core_02.040_stewardship_capacity_principles.md#164-document-at-action) (2.040 §16.4) (*the principle that the act makes its own Act Record*).
- Read with: [CS-21.305 §10 inspectable attributable action](corpus_systems/cs_21.305_critical_system_stewardship.md#cs-21305-103-inspectable-attributable-action) (*default logging contract for mixed crews; not a Standing-Record substitute*).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: when an action materially affects sentients or shared systems, it must be possible to tell who or what is responsible for it well enough to review, challenge, and correct it.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** A materially relevant action whose origin, responsible actor, and answerability chain can be traced to identifiable systems, agents, or sentients with enough fidelity for audit, review, correction, and remedy. Covered actions include those affecting sentients, shared infrastructure, resource systems, or the info-sphere.
  - **Out of scope:** Attribution demands beyond what material impact, risk, and constitutional review require; low-significance actions that do not need persistent identity linkage so long as proportional attribution capability still exists where harm, dispute, or abuse must be investigated.
<a id="attributable-action-constitutional-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) — asks whether materially significant actions can be traced to identifiable responsible actors strongly enough for answerability to be real in practice.

    **Primary assessment:** Check whether attribution survives delegation, layered architectures, proxy operation, vendor chains, and time-separated workflows; whether the Act Record identifies who acted, under what authority, and with what materially relevant effect; and whether the trace is usable for review, challenge, and remedy rather than merely existing on paper.
  - **Secondary measure:** [Auditability](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#auditability), [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability), [Transparency](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#transparency), and [Attribution Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#attribution-integrity) — co-measures that can defeat attributable action even when a nominal log or identity field exists.

    **Secondary assessment:** When evaluating the primary trace, assess whether missing records, delegated opacity, retained ambiguity, or inaccessible records make the action effectively unattributable in practice.
<a id="attributable-action-constitutional-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** It is non-compliant to route materially significant action through structures that prevent reliable identification of the responsible system, agent, or sentient where review, challenge, correction, or remedy may be required.
  - **Secondary failure:** A system claims actions are attributable because identifiers or logs exist somewhere, but the trace cannot practically support audit, contest, or answerability under real conditions.

---

<a id="attribution-integrity-constitutional"></a>

#### Attribution Integrity

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Principles:
  - [Chapter Two §18 Governance under Stewardship Discipline](core_02.050_governance_incentive_application.md#18-governance-under-stewardship-discipline) (2.050 §18);
  - [Subcore 8-B §4.4](subcore_08.020_system_alignment_certification_evaluation.md#44-data-types-and-handling-evaluation) (S8-B §4.4);
  - [Subcore 8-F §3](subcore_08.060_system_alignment_certification_records_recertification.md#3-system-certification-record) (S8-F §3); and **
  - [Subcore 4-A — Data Types](subcore_04.050_data_types.md#subcore-4-a-data-types) and [CS-4.305](corpus_systems/cs_04.305_information_types_and_handling.md#cs-4305-information-types-and-handling) (*Information types and handling*)** where attributable action or audit-level responsibility is materially implicated.
- Read with:
  - [Attributable Action](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#attributable-action),
  - [Accountability](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#accountability),
  - [Auditability](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#auditability),
  - [Record Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#record-integrity),
  - [Transparency](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#transparency),
  - [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability), and
  - [Truth (Constitutional Constraint)](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#truth-constitutional-constraint).
- Read with: [CS-21.305 §10 inspectable attributable action](corpus_systems/cs_21.305_critical_system_stewardship.md#cs-21305-103-inspectable-attributable-action) (*default logging contract for mixed crews; internals are not a standing-measurement exemption*).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: attribution has integrity only when responsibility cannot be hidden, denied, tampered with, or blurred into ambiguity.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** The constitutional requirement that attribution chains for materially significant actions remain transparent enough, auditable enough, and stable enough to resist obscuring responsibility through indirection, delegation, system complexity, tampering, repudiation, or ambiguity.
  - **Out of scope:** A demand for one universal technical mechanism, immutable identity exposure in every context, or stronger attribution than proportionality, privacy, and protected-boundary rules permit.
<a id="attribution-integrity-constitutional-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) and [Oversight measurement family](subcore_03.055_apex_oversight_leg.md#measuring-oversight-preamble-measurement-family) — used together to test whether the attribution chain remains answerable and reviewable in practice.

    **Primary assessment:** Evaluate whether responsibility can be obscured by role fragmentation, handoffs, proxy actors, undocumented overrides, layered ownership, or inaccessible records; whether the chain can be audited without dependence on self-serving operator claims alone; and whether tampering, repudiation, or retained ambiguity would defeat reliable attribution.
  - **Secondary measure:** [Proxy Divergence](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#proxy-divergence), [Auditability](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#auditability), and [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability) — co-measures that expose symbolic attribution that does not function under review.

    **Secondary assessment:** When operators rely on nominal identity fields, nominal logs, or contractual role labels, distinguish real attribution integrity from paper-only traceability.
<a id="attribution-integrity-constitutional-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** It is non-compliant to obscure responsibility through indirection, delegation, or system complexity, or to create conditions where materially significant actions cannot be reliably attributed.
  - **Secondary failure:** Attribution records or mechanisms are present but vulnerable to tampering, repudiation, or unresolved ambiguity such that reliable review, correction, or remedy cannot depend on them.

---

<a id="record-integrity-constitutional"></a>

#### Record Integrity

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Principles:
  - [Chapter Two §16.4 Document at Action](core_02.040_stewardship_capacity_principles.md#164-document-at-action) (2.040 §16.4) (*the act makes its own Act Record*);
  - [Chapter Two §18 Governance under Stewardship Discipline](core_02.050_governance_incentive_application.md#18-governance-under-stewardship-discipline) (2.050 §18);
  - [Chapter Six §9 Act Records and Attributable Handoffs](core_06.000_functional_independence_separation_of_duties.md#9-act-records-and-attributable-handoffs) (6.000 §9);
  - [Subcore 8-F §3](subcore_08.060_system_alignment_certification_records_recertification.md#3-system-certification-record) (S8-F §3);
  - [Subcore 10-B §6.4 Versioning](subcore_10.020_standing_assessment.md#64-versioning) (S10-B §6.4) and [Subcore 10-B §7 Record Custody and Opening Authority](subcore_10.020_standing_assessment.md#7-record-custody-and-opening-authority) (S10-B §7);
  - [Chapter Sixteen §5 Forum Cases: Intakes, Seats, Records, and Contests](core_16.000_forum.md#5-forum-cases-intakes-seats-records-and-contests) (22.000 §5); and
  - [Chapter Twenty-Three §7 Procedural Test 3](core_23.000_amendment_ratification.md#7-procedural-test-3--authority-chain-and-custody-validity) (29.000 §7) (*the records of a proposed amendment*).
- Read with:
  - [Attributable Action](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#attributable-action),
  - [Attribution Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#attribution-integrity),
  - [Materially Binding Act Record](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#materially-binding-act-record),
  - [Record Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#record-seat),
  - [Record Custodian](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#record-custodian),
  - [Evidence Preservation](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#evidence-preservation),
  - [Auditability](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#auditability),
  - [Transparency](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#transparency),
  - [Publication Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#publication-integrity) (*the proposal before the decision*), and
  - [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: an official record can be relied on only if you can tell who made it, what it said at each point, that nothing was quietly changed or dropped, and that the sentients entitled to check it can reach it. A completed official record is never rewritten. Changes are added as new versions, and the old ones stay.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** The requirement that an official record a constitutional process relies on to decide, review, challenge, or correct a materially significant matter stays reliable as an official record. That covers an Act Record, a System Certification Record, a Standing Record, a Forum Case Record, and any other process record. It has six parts:
    - **Attributable:** each entry and each change shows who made it, in what role, and under what authority.
    - **Versioned:** every change is a new version, and nothing is changed in place (see *Not edited in place* below).
    - **Preserved:** the official record and its prior versions are kept for as long as review, contest, remedy, or standing assessment can need them.
    - **Reconstructable:** the act or decision, the evidence relied on, the handoffs, refusals, and objections, and the clocks can be rebuilt from the official record and its links.
    - **Reachable:** authorized reviewers can get to it through the official record path, and the public can as disclosure rules require. Privacy, confidentiality, and security rules decide who sees what. They never justify erasing the trail or making it unusable.
    - **Independent:** the sentient or system whose act is recorded does not control the record of that act. Its own logs, signatures, and attestations are inputs, not the official record.
  - A process may keep the official record as one piece or as a linked set. No duplicate official record is required.
  - **Care scales** with the materiality of the act and, for a System, its class ([Subcore 8-B §3 System Class Evaluation](subcore_08.020_system_alignment_certification_evaluation.md#3-system-class-evaluation)). An official record behind a materially binding act, or behind a higher-class System, needs custody and a version trail that a reviewer can trust without taking the operator's word. An official record behind a low-materiality matter may use a simpler trail, such as a plain change log. No scale removes attribution, the version trail, or the rule against editing a completed official record in place, for any official record a materially binding act rests on.
  - **Out of scope:** A demand for one technical mechanism, for records that can never change, for identity exposure in every context, or for disclosure beyond what the disclosure rules require. A correction made as a visible new version is not alteration. What a given record must contain stays with its owner (for an Act Record, [Chapter Six §9 Act Records and Attributable Handoffs](core_06.000_functional_independence_separation_of_duties.md#9-act-records-and-attributable-handoffs)). Evidence that is not itself an official record stays with [Evidence Preservation](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#evidence-preservation).
- **Not edited in place**
  - **Open and completed:** While the process that keeps an official record is still running, the official record may be amended, and each amendment is a new version that shows what changed. The official record is completed when the process enters its decision or closing entry. From then on the existing entries are not changed.
  - **Four kinds of later version:** A later version may update the official record going forward, correct it back to when it was wrong, expire it for a completed decision context, or supersede it for a new decision context. Standing Records already work this way ([Subcore 10-B §6.4 Versioning](subcore_10.020_standing_assessment.md#64-versioning)).
  - **What a later version states:** It says what changed, who made the change, under what authority, on what verification materials, and at whose request.
  - **Old versions stay:** The version that was relied on remains reachable, marked as corrected or superseded, with the dates it was in force. A correction does not hide the error it corrects.
  - **New events are added, not written over:** Operating evidence, a later finding, a challenge and its result, and a later review attach to the completed official record as linked entries or as a new official record that links back to it.
  - **True dates:** Each version carries the date it was made. A version is never back-dated to look as if it was there earlier.
  - **Who may make a version:** Only the office that holds the official record, acting under published authority ([Record Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#record-seat); [Record Custodian](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#record-custodian)). The sentient or system whose act is recorded, and anyone in its control line, may ask for a change but cannot make it. The request is logged whether or not it is acted on.
  - **Limits on what is shown apply to the view, not the official record:** Privacy, safety, or security limits change what a reader sees. The full version is kept for authorized reviewers, and the shown version says that something is withheld and why. Where a higher rule requires material to be removed, the removal is itself recorded: what kind of material, under what authority, and when.
  - **A challenge does not freeze or erase the official record:** It stays inspectable, its prior versions stay, and its status shows that it is under challenge ([Subcore 10-B §7 Record Custody and Opening Authority](subcore_10.020_standing_assessment.md#7-record-custody-and-opening-authority)).
<a id="record-integrity-constitutional-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) and [Oversight measurement family](subcore_03.055_apex_oversight_leg.md#measuring-oversight-preamble-measurement-family) — used together to test whether an official record can still be relied on by someone who is not its keeper.

    **Primary assessment:** Ask whether a reviewer who is not the operator can rebuild, from the official record and its links alone, what was decided, by whom, on what evidence, and what changed between versions. Check who can enter or change the official record, whether a change leaves a visible trace, whether prior versions are kept and reachable, whether custody is clear, and whether the official record is tamper-evident where feasible. Read the answer against the materiality of the act and, for a System, its class.
  - **Secondary measure:** [Proxy Divergence](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#proxy-divergence), [Auditability](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#auditability), and [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability) — co-measures that expose integrity that exists on paper only.

    **Secondary assessment:** Tell real integrity from paper-only integrity: a log that can be edited without trace, a version label with no kept prior version, a fingerprint or hash with no clear custody, or an official record split across places so that no one can rebuild it.
<a id="record-integrity-constitutional-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** To alter in place, back-date, replace, delete, withhold, split, relabel, or make unreachable an official record or any prior version of it, so that a reviewer cannot rebuild what was decided, by whom, and on what basis; or to let the subject of an act control the record of that act.
  - **Secondary failure:** Records exist, but their integrity is nominal: they can change without a visible trace, prior versions are not kept, authorship or custody is unclear, or they can be reached only through the subject of the act.

---

<a id="publication-integrity-constitutional"></a>

#### Publication Integrity

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream:
  - [Chapter Twenty-Three §6 Procedural Test 2](core_23.000_amendment_ratification.md#6-procedural-test-2--procedural-validity-and-publication-integrity) (29.000 §6) (*a proposed amendment*) and [§3.2 Proposal, Hearing, and Comment Window](core_23.000_amendment_ratification.md#32-proposal-hearing-and-comment-window) (29.000 §3.2);
  - [Subcore 7-B §6 Timing, Response, and No Veto](subcore_07.020_public_policy_office.md#6-timing-response-and-no-veto) (*an evaluation published before the decision*); and
  - any other process that decides a proposed [materially binding act](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#materially-binding-act) after a public response.
- Read with:
  - [Record Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#record-integrity) (*the official record after the decision*),
  - [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability),
  - [Due Process](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#due-process),
  - [Transparency](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#transparency),
  - [Subcore 3-C](subcore_03.030_burden_traceability_verification.md#subcore-3-c-burden-of-proof-traceability-and-verification) (S3-C) traceability, and
  - [CJS-0.105 §4 Time-Window Names](corpus_joint_structure/cjs_00.105_registry_and_reading_rules.md#cjs-0105-4-time-window-names).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: before a binding decision that the public may respond to, the real proposal has to be out in the open: the exact text, what it changes and for whom, what it is expected to do, and how to object, early enough that those affected can actually answer. Shadow drafts and last-minute rewrites do not count. Record Integrity covers official records afterward; this covers the proposal beforehand.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** The requirement that a proposal for a materially binding act, where the process that decides it is open to response from the public or from the sentients it affects, is published so that they can understand it and answer it before the decision. It has five parts:
    - **Complete:** the publication states the exact text or terms proposed, the scope of the proposal, the provisions or rules it changes, who is likely to be affected and how, its expected effects, and the route to respond and to contest it. The owning process may add elements (for an amendment, the justification in [Chapter Twenty-Three §5 Burden of Justification and Public Traceability](core_23.000_amendment_ratification.md#5-burden-of-justification-and-public-traceability)).
    - **Specific:** it is detailed enough that an affected reader can tell what would change for them and can raise a concrete objection.
    - **Timely:** it is published at least the named response window before the decision. The owning process names the window and scales it with materiality; the window may not be shorter than any floor the owning process or [CJS-0.105 §4 Time-Window Names](corpus_joint_structure/cjs_00.105_registry_and_reading_rules.md#cjs-0105-4-time-window-names) sets.
    - **On the official record path:** it is published on the official record path the decision will rest on, and kept under [Record Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#record-integrity), so that what was proposed, when, and in what wording can be rebuilt later.
    - **Current:** every later change to the proposal is published as a dated, recorded change. A material change restarts the response window from its date, unless the owning process sets a shorter window for the change that still allows a real answer.
  - **Care scales** with the materiality of the act. A low-materiality matter may use a short notice and a short window. No scale removes the exact text, the recorded changes, or the route to respond for an act that is materially binding.
  - **Out of scope:** Whether the proposal is right on the merits, who may decide it, and how responses are weighed. Those stay with the owning process. Emergency measures follow their own rules ([Chapter Seventeen §5.1 Emergency Measures and Continuation Burden](core_17.000_forum_families_escalation_timeliness_support.md#51-emergency-measures-and-continuation-burden)); this requirement applies in full to any decision to continue them or make them permanent. Material withheld under privacy, safety, or security rules is described but not shown, as [Record Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#record-integrity) provides for the view of an official record.
<a id="publication-integrity-constitutional-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) and [Participation measurement family](subcore_03.060_apex_participation_leg.md#measuring-participation-preamble-measurement-family) — used together to test whether those affected could know and answer the proposal before it was decided.

    **Primary assessment:** Compare what was decided with what was published, and when. Check that every element was published, that the text decided matches a published version, that each change was recorded with its date, and that the response window ran in full from the last material change. Ask whether an affected reader could have told from the publication alone what would change for them.
  - **Secondary measure:** [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability), [Transparency](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#transparency), and [Timely Resolution](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#timely-resolution) — co-measures that expose notice that exists on paper only.

    **Secondary assessment:** Tell real notice from paper notice: a publication buried where those affected cannot find it, a summary that hides the operative text, a window that runs over a period when response is impractical, or a stream of small changes that together change the proposal without restarting the window.
<a id="publication-integrity-constitutional-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** To decide a materially binding act on a proposal that was not published, on wording that differs materially from every published version, or before the response window has run; or to keep a shadow draft that is the real proposal.
  - **Secondary failure:** A publication exists, but it is incomplete, too vague to answer, hard for those affected to reach, or changed without a recorded, dated version.

---

<a id="contestability"></a>

#### Contestability

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Principles: [Constitutional Tetrad](core_00.000_preamble.md#constitutional-tetrad) (participation leg); [13. Constitutional Collision Resolution Process](core_02.020_interaction_interpretation.md#13-constitutional-collision-resolution-process) (review, escalation, and emergency proportionality read-with); [2.1. Non-Negotiable Constraints: Safety and Truth](core_02.000_values_principles.md#21-non-negotiable-principle-constraints-safety-and-truth) (where contestability operationalizes Truth / Safety).
- Read with: the
  - [Accountability, Contestability, Adjudication and Dispute Resolution, Collective Accountability Failure, and Force Majeure cluster](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#accountability),
  - [Accountability](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#accountability),
  - [Auditability](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#auditability),
  - [Transparency](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#transparency),
  - [Redress and Remediation](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#redress-and-remediation),
  - [Publication Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#publication-integrity) (*being able to answer a proposal before it is decided*), and
  - [Subcore 14-A §4.1 Rights-Floor Waivers and Unconstitutional Contract Terms](subcore_14.010_misconduct_pattern_applications.md#41-rights-floor-waivers-and-unconstitutional-contract-terms) (S14-A §4.1).
- Downstream: [Chapter Twenty-Three §8 Procedural Test 4](core_23.000_amendment_ratification.md#8-procedural-test-4--contestability-and-independent-review-validity) (29.000 §8) (*a proposed amendment*).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: parties affected by a system or decision must have a real way to challenge it, get it reviewed, and seek correction and remedy. No one can be made to give up that right as the price of taking part, and where a rule calls for independent review, it has to actually happen.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** The practical ability of affected parties to challenge system behavior or decisions, obtain review, seek correction, and use working remedy pathways to [Redress and Remediation](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#redress-and-remediation). It includes:
    - **No waiver as the price of taking part:** access to a process, service, or participation is never conditioned on giving up a right to challenge, to audit, or to standing that this Constitution protects;
    - **Independent review where required:** where a rule requires review by someone independent of the decision-maker, that review takes place and is not skipped, suppressed, or reduced to a formality.
  - **Out of scope:** mere complaint inbox not claimed as a review pathway, or ordinary feedback outside challenge, review, and correction.
<a id="contestability-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) — checks whether affected parties have a practical way to challenge a system or decision, obtain review, and seek correction.

    **Primary assessment:** Check that challenge mechanisms work, are accessible, can be [audited](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#auditability), and are [trustworthy](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#trustworthiness).
  - **Secondary measure:** [Accountability](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#accountability), [Auditability](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#auditability), [Transparency](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#transparency), and [Redress and Remediation](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#redress-and-remediation) — co-measures that can defeat contestability even when formal challenge menus exist.
