<a id="protected-reporting-semi-independent"></a>

#### Protected Reporting and Anti-Retaliation

**Semi-independent context** (component definitions may still operate outside joint-invocation scope):

- **Joint invocation:** where protected reporting, whistleblowing, retaliation against reporters, or interference with reporting access is material.
- **Scope:** where joint invocation is met, the Accountability home for the channels through which constitutional violations, safety failures, and systemic harms are brought to light — [Protected Reporting (Whistleblowing)](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#protected-reporting-whistleblowing) for disclosures shielded from reprisal, and [Protected Reporting Retaliation and Access Interference](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#protected-reporting-retaliation-and-access-interference) for prohibited responses and access-blocking tactics. These definitions protect the information pathways essential to [Accountability](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#accountability), [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability), and [Auditability](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#auditability).

**Topic group members.** This group comprises:
- [Protected Reporting (Whistleblowing)](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#protected-reporting-whistleblowing);
- [Protected Reporting Retaliation and Access Interference](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#protected-reporting-retaliation-and-access-interference).

*Measurements (family routing):* Measured under the Accountability measurement family. Find the concrete measures on the member definitions below.

---

<a id="protected-reporting-whistleblowing"></a>

#### Protected Reporting (Whistleblowing)

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Principles: [2.1. Non-Negotiable Constraints: Safety and Truth](core_02.000_values_principles.md#21-non-negotiable-principle-constraints-safety-and-truth) and [Subcore 8-B §4 Whole-System Certification Evaluation](subcore_08.020_system_alignment_certification_evaluation.md#4-whole-system-certification-evaluation) (S8-B §4) through [Subcore 8-B §6 Rights-Floor and Domain Evaluations](subcore_08.020_system_alignment_certification_evaluation.md#6-rights-floor-and-domain-evaluations), where reporting supplies safety, truth, accountability, or material-risk evidence.
- Cluster component: [the dependent cluster *Protected Reporting and Anti-Retaliation*](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#protected-reporting-and-anti-retaliation).
- Read with:
  - [Accountability](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#accountability),
  - [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability),
  - [Auditability](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#auditability),
  - [Evidence Preservation](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#evidence-preservation),
  - [Transparency](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#transparency),
  - [Redress and Remediation](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#redress-and-remediation), and
  - [Protected Reporting Retaliation and Access Interference](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#protected-reporting-retaliation-and-access-interference).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: a sentient may safely report or escalate a reasonable concern about constitutional violations, serious harm, safety or truth failures, corruption, capture, abuse, retaliation, or blocked review.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** A disclosure or escalation by a sentient, stakeholder, worker, participant, auditor, or similar actor about a reasonable suspicion of constitutional non-compliance, material harm, a safety or truth failure, corruption, capture, abuse, retaliation, or obstruction of required review.
  - **Out of scope:** ordinary workplace feedback, suggestion box, or personal grievance not claiming constitutional protected-reporting protection.
<a id="protected-reporting-whistleblowing-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) — checks whether sentients can safely report serious concerns and whether those reports are received, preserved, and reviewed.

    **Primary assessment:** Look at what the report does, not just what it is called. Protected Reporting includes internal escalation, external reporting through authorized oversight or forum pathways, [Evidence Preservation](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#evidence-preservation), and Good Faith participation in an audit or review.
  - **Secondary measure:** [Accountability](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#accountability), [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability), [Evidence Preservation](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#evidence-preservation), and [Auditability](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#auditability) — co-measures that can defeat reporting even when formal channels exist.

    **Secondary assessment:** A reporter does not need to state the legal issue perfectly. Protection applies when the reporter had a reasonable basis and used a channel proportionate to the circumstances.
<a id="protected-reporting-whistleblowing-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** Suppressing, burdening, burying, or refusing to receive Protected Reporting; treating Good Faith reporting as misconduct only because it exposes an institutional or system failure; or a protected-reporting label or channel that provides no real protection, or that is used to identify, expose, punish, intimidate, or otherwise retaliate against a reporter or anyone who supports the report.
  - **Secondary failure:** Requiring silence as a condition of access to remedy when accountability, safety, or truth duties require disclosure.

---

<a id="protected-reporting-retaliation-and-access-interference"></a>

#### Protected Reporting Retaliation and Access Interference

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Principles: [13. Constitutional Collision Resolution Process](core_02.020_interaction_interpretation.md#13-constitutional-collision-resolution-process) and [Chapter Two §18 Governance under Stewardship Discipline](core_02.050_governance_incentive_application.md#18-governance-under-stewardship-discipline) (2.050 §18), where retaliation or access interference distorts challenge and accountability pathways.
- Cluster component: [the dependent cluster *Protected Reporting and Anti-Retaliation*](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#protected-reporting-and-anti-retaliation).
- Read with:
  - [Protected Reporting (Whistleblowing)](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#protected-reporting-whistleblowing),
  - [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability),
  - [Accountability](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#accountability),
  - [Evidence Preservation](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#evidence-preservation),
  - [Procedural Fairness](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#procedural-fairness),
  - [Redress and Remediation](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#redress-and-remediation), and
  - [System Capture](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#system-capture).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: no actor may punish, threaten, exclude, discredit, delay, or block access for a sentient because that sentient reported a concern, preserved evidence, joined an audit, made a challenge, or sought review or remedy.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** Any retaliation, threat, penalty, exclusion, damage to reputation, denial of access, manipulation of records, or obstruction of process materially connected to Protected Reporting, [Evidence Preservation](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#evidence-preservation), audit participation, challenge, review, or access to remediation.
  - **Out of scope:** ordinary performance management, scheduling, or resource allocation with no material connection to Protected Reporting or review participation.
<a id="protected-reporting-retaliation-and-access-interference-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) — *Do reward structures, market power, and answerability keep duties real?*

    **Primary assessment:** Include indirect and delayed retaliation. Examples include:
    - changing work assignments;
    - blocking forum access;
    - degrading credentials or standing;
    - throttling a platform account;
    - using dependency as pressure;
    - misusing confidentiality; and
    - delaying procedure to exhaust the reporter or claimant.
  - **Secondary measure:** [Protected Reporting (Whistleblowing)](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#protected-reporting-whistleblowing), [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability), [Procedural Fairness](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#procedural-fairness), and [System Capture](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#system-capture) — co-measures that can defeat anti-retaliation discipline even when policies exist on paper.

    **Secondary assessment:** Treat interference with access as closely related to retaliation when it actually prevents reporting, review, or remedy.
<a id="protected-reporting-retaliation-and-access-interference-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** An adverse action or obstruction of access materially caused by Protected Reporting or participation in review. An anti-retaliation label or policy that provides no real protection, causes or enables retaliation, or is used to hide it is also non-compliant.
  - **Secondary failure:** A supposedly neutral process change that foreseeably blocks Protected Reporting; or a remedy channel that exists on paper but retaliation makes unusable.

---

<a id="subversion"></a>

#### Subversion

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Principles: [Chapter Two §7 Freedom (Bounded Agency)](core_02.000_values_principles.md#7-freedom-bounded-agency) (2.000 §7); [Chapter Two §7.3 Dissent and Peaceful Protest](core_02.000_values_principles.md#73-dissent-and-peaceful-protest) (2.000 §7.3) (dissent is not subversion).
- Canonical owner: this definition states what subversion is. [Chapter Fourteen §5](core_14.000_misconduct_designation.md#5-criteria-set-for-anti-constitutional-misconduct-designation--accountability-at-constitutional-scale) (20.000 §5) for criteria 5 and 6 (*Insurrection or material forcible subversion* and *Operative nullification*), and [Subcore 14-A §7.1](subcore_14.010_misconduct_pattern_applications.md#71-concentration-based-subversion-criteria-interaction) (S14-A §7.1) apply it to misconduct designation. They do not redefine it.
- Rights-Floor read-with: **[Article XI-D](core_04.010_rights_part_b.md#xi-d-dissent-and-peaceful-protest) (*Assembly, Dissent, and Peaceful Protest*)**.
- Read with:
  - [Coercion and Manipulation](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#coercion-and-manipulation),
  - [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability),
  - [System Capture](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#system-capture),
  - [Anti-Constitutional Misconduct Review](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#anti-constitutional-misconduct-review),
  - [Necessity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#necessity), and
  - [Proportionality](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#proportionality).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: subversion is attacking constitutional systems by force, coercion, or taking power that was never assigned — or by making their process and remedies unusable in practice. Disagreeing with those systems, and working openly and lawfully to change them, is not subversion.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** Conduct, or a material attempt at conduct, that aims to overthrow, suspend, replace, or defeat constitutional authority, process, or remedies by:
    - **force**;
    - [coercion](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#coercion-and-manipulation);
    - **usurpation** — exercising constitutional authority that was never lawfully assigned to the actor; or
    - making constitutional process or remedies unusable in practice, so that they remain only on paper (see [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability)).
  - **In scope:** An attempt counts even if it fails, where it is material.
  - **Out of scope:**
    - disagreement with this Constitution or with authority acting under it;
    - peaceful, lawful advocacy to amend this Constitution or to change who holds authority;
    - dissent, peaceful protest, and civil disobedience protected by **Article XI-D** (*Assembly, Dissent, and Peaceful Protest*); and
    - [Necessity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#necessity)-bounded emergency measures justified under **Chapter Two** and **Chapter Twenty-Three §5.1** (*Emergency Measures and Continuation Burden*).
<a id="subversion-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) — checks whether constitutional process and remedies stay usable and answerable.

    **Primary assessment:**
    - Ask what the conduct does, not what it is called. Look at its purpose and its effect on constitutional authority, process, and remedies.
    - Separate force, coercion, or usurpation from the second route (making process or remedies unusable). Each needs its own proof.
    - Disruption, inconvenience, offense, unpopularity, or pressure on authority is not subversion by itself.
  - **Secondary measure:** [Coercion and Manipulation](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#coercion-and-manipulation), [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability), and [System Capture](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#system-capture) — co-measures that show whether pressure, lost challenge pathways, or captured control are doing the subverting.

    **Secondary assessment:** When evaluating the primary trace, test whether a formally lawful step is being used to reach one of the four routes above. Test whether the actor is also claiming protected dissent for conduct that is separable and independently subversive.
<a id="subversion-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** Subversion of constitutional systems is non-compliant. It is not protected agency under [Freedom (Bounded Agency)](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#freedom-bounded-agency).
  - **Secondary failure:** Treating dissent or peaceful protest as subversion, or using the label to limit them outside [Chapter Two §7.1 Limitation Discipline](core_02.000_values_principles.md#71-limitation-discipline), is also non-compliant.

---

<a id="assembly-collective-organization-institutional-formation-semi-independent"></a>
<a id="governance-architecture-oversight-decentralization-and-concentration-cluster"></a>

#### Governance Architecture, Decentralization, and Concentration

**Semi-independent context** (component definitions may still operate outside joint-invocation scope):

- **Joint invocation:** where governance architecture, functional independence, material control relationships, interested-party relationships, decentralization scale, market structure, or material concentration of authority is material.
- **Scope:** where joint invocation is met, the Accountability home for how authority is organized, constrained, and separated through distributed, accountable authority structures.
- **Owner floor:** implements [Chapter Two §11 Market Structure](core_02.010_values_principles_continuity_aim.md#11-market-structure) and constrains [System Capture](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#system-capture).
- **Principle-layer interface:** read with [Chapter Two §17.4 Aligned Self-Organization](core_02.040_stewardship_capacity_principles.md#174-aligned-self-organization). That principle explains how sentient-initiated and community-initiated constitutional work receives a real procedural path without allowing incumbents to monopolize initiative or review. It is not a grant of governing, enforcement, coercive, or decision-making authority and is not an additional definition in this group.

**Topic group members.** This group comprises:
- [Governance](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#governance) — structures, rules, and processes by which systems are directed and held accountable.
- [Material Control Line](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#material-control-line) — the real chain of practical power that can materially affect a required seat's independent judgment or action on a particular act.
- [Interested Party](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#interested-party) — for a particular matter, a sentient, institution, or system with a material stake in the outcome, and anyone, or any system, acting for it or inside its Material Control Line.
- [Initiating Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#initiating-seat) — the role that requests, proposes, operates, claims, or otherwise begins a particular act.
- [Verify-or-Authorize Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#verify-or-authorize-seat) — the role that checks evidence and authority against the governing standard and determines whether the act may proceed.
- [Record Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#record-seat) — the role that enters, amends, holds, preserves, and publishes the official record of the determination.
- [Contest Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contest-seat) — the role that receives and reviews a challenge, orders correction or limits where authorized, and routes issues outside its authority.
- [Materially Binding Act](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#materially-binding-act) — an attributable act under constitutional, adopted, or delegated authority that materially creates, changes, confirms, suspends, or ends a right, duty, status, standing, permission, allocation, record, or operating state, and that others must follow or may rely on as settled.
- [Materially Binding Act Record](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#materially-binding-act-record) — the attributable, versioned official record for a materially binding act; short form: **Act Record**.
- [Decentralization](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#decentralization) — placement of authority at the least centralized scale that can satisfy binding requirements.
- [Standardization](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#standardization) — the default of one common, published, openly available form for terms, interfaces, procedures, and like cases wherever no binding reason supports variation.
- [Market Structure](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#market-structure) — principle-layer discipline for contestable productive conditions.
- [Market Concentration Threshold](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#market-concentration-threshold) — operational trigger for heightened review when material concentration reaches problematic levels.

*Measurements (family routing):* Measured under the Accountability measurement family. Find the concrete measures on [Material Control Line](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#material-control-line), [Interested Party](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#interested-party), the four seat definitions, [Market Structure](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#market-structure), and [Market Concentration Threshold](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#market-concentration-threshold) below.

<a id="governance"></a>

#### Governance

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Principles:
  - [Constitutional Tetrad](core_00.000_preamble.md#constitutional-tetrad) (
  - [material stake](core_00.000_preamble.md#material-stake) scaling);
  - [Preamble §3.2 governance-layer discipline](core_00.000_preamble.md#32-governance-layers);
  - [Chapter Two §18 Governance under Stewardship Discipline](core_02.050_governance_incentive_application.md#18-governance-under-stewardship-discipline) (2.050 §18) (governance capture discipline).
- Cluster component: [Governance Architecture, Oversight, Dependency, Decentralization, Concentration, Market Structure, and Exit-Path Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#governance-architecture-decentralization-and-concentration); [Stewardship, Governance Discipline, and Shared-System Capacity](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#defc2-stewardship-governance-discipline-and-shared-system-capacity).
- Read with:
  - [Stewardship](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#stewardship),
  - [Chapter Two §17.4 Aligned Self-Organization](core_02.040_stewardship_capacity_principles.md#174-aligned-self-organization) (2.040 §17.4),
  - [Short-Horizon Governance Defect](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#short-horizon-governance-defect),
  - [Classification-Scaled Governance](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#classification-scaled-governance),
  - [Oversight](subcore_03.055_apex_oversight_leg.md#oversight-constitutional),
  - [Accountability](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#accountability),
  - [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability),
  - [Proportionality](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#proportionality),
  - [Necessity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#necessity), and
  - [System Capture](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#system-capture).
- Layer: route between **Constitutional Contract Layer (CCL)** and **Stakeholder System Participation (SSP)** under [Preamble §3.2](core_00.000_preamble.md#32-governance-layers).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: Governance is how a system assigns and limits power, makes decisions, changes over time, and holds decision-makers answerable. More consequential power requires stronger oversight, accountability, and review.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** The structures, rules, distribution of authority, and processes used to direct, constrain, change, and hold systems and institutions [Accountable](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#accountability). This includes:
    - technical;
    - organizational;
    - economic; and
    - socio-technical layers that materially affect sentients.

    At principle layer, **Governance** is subordinate to [Stewardship](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#stewardship) when authorized structures, incentives, or procedures would otherwise allow foreseeable constitutional misalignment, Rights-Floor degradation, or short-term optimization that defeats durable alignment. **Chapter Twenty-Seven** and the **Constitutional Contract Layer** continue to own operative authorization requirements.
  - **Out of scope:** private club bylaws, brand style guides, or informal social coordination that do not direct or constrain systems this Constitution governs.
<a id="governance-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) and [Timeliness measurement family](subcore_03.065_apex_timeliness_leg.md#measuring-timeliness) — used alongside any other relevant measures to assess responsibility and whether action happens without harmful delay.

    **Primary assessment:** Apply [Classification-Scaled Governance](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#classification-scaled-governance), [Oversight](subcore_03.055_apex_oversight_leg.md#oversight-constitutional), [Accountability](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#accountability), [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability), [Proportionality](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#proportionality), [Necessity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#necessity), and [System Capture](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#system-capture). Check whether authority is distributed, bounded, and reviewable in proportion to the [material stake](core_00.000_preamble.md#material-stake). Identify [Short-Horizon Governance Defect](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#short-horizon-governance-defect) when Governance optimizes immediate results without checking them again against medium- and long-term constitutional outcomes. Designated incorporated Governance implementation may add detail but must not narrow this definition.
<a id="governance-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - A claimed Governance arrangement remains in scope for evaluation when it seeks to seize, displace, bypass, or materially obstruct authority assigned through a valid constitutional authorization pathway. This rule does not prohibit challenge, review, amendment, replacement, or emergency action carried out through constitutionally authorized challenge, review, amendment, replacement, or emergency pathways.
  - **Primary failure:**
    - Governance arrangements that materially:
      - negate required [Oversight](subcore_03.055_apex_oversight_leg.md#oversight-constitutional), [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability), or [Accountability](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#accountability);
      - produce or entrench [System Capture](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#system-capture) without mitigation when such capture is materially prohibited;
      - normalize permanent emergency;
      - suppress correction; or
      - entrench capture while preserving procedure only in name.
    - a Governance label or formal structure that has no real effect in the situation being evaluated.
    - attempting to seize, displace, bypass, or materially obstruct constitutionally assigned authority, even when the attempted subversion does not succeed.

<a id="material-control-line"></a>

#### Material Control Line

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream:
  - [Chapter Six §4 Independence, Conflict, and Control Lines](core_06.000_functional_independence_separation_of_duties.md#4-independence-conflict-and-control-lines) (6.000 §4);
  - [Subcore 10-B §7 Record Custody and Opening Authority](subcore_10.020_standing_assessment.md#7-record-custody-and-opening-authority) (S10-B §7); Chapter Twenty-Two forum anti-self-judging;
  - **CI-6.205 §2** (*Functional Separation Lanes*);
  - **CI-21.205 §6** (*Seat catalog*);
  - **CI-6.210** (*Conflict, integrity, anti-capture, and anti-corruption*); and
  - **CF-16.425** (*Integrity safeguards, anti-capture operations, and anti-self-judging support*).
- Cluster component: [Governance Architecture, Decentralization, and Concentration](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#governance-architecture-decentralization-and-concentration).
- Read with:
  - [Governance](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#governance),
  - [Accountability](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#accountability),
  - [Oversight](subcore_03.055_apex_oversight_leg.md#oversight-constitutional),
  - [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability),
  - [Attribution Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#attribution-integrity),
  - [System Capture](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#system-capture),
  - [Materiality](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#materiality),
  - [Domain-Specific Materiality](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#domain-specific-materiality) (which borrows the idea of common practical control to count Operating Parties as one operation, but has its own test), and
  - [Dependency](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#dependency).
- Layer: applies within both the **Constitutional Contract Layer (CCL)** and **Stakeholder System Participation (SSP)** under [Preamble §3.2](core_00.000_preamble.md#32-governance-layers).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: a Material Control Line is the real chain of power around a particular act. It includes the sentients, offices, or systems that can meaningfully direct, overrule, reward, punish, remove, delay, or suppress the work, even when an organization chart calls the seat independent.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** For a particular act, the set of sentients, offices, systems, or governing bodies connected through practical power that could materially affect a required seat's independent judgment or action. The power may be direct or indirect, formal or informal, and may include authority or leverage to:
    - direct, approve, veto, or override the outcome;
    - select or suppress evidence, or alter the governing standard for the act;
    - reward, penalize, remove, or replace a seat holder because of the act;
    - control access, resources, credentials, funding, data, or systems in a way that can affect the act; or
    - prevent or materially delay routing, challenge, record preservation, correction, or remedy.

    A Material Control Line may run upward, downward, or across formal reporting structures, including through affiliates, contractors, funding relationships, technical administration, proxies, or automated systems.
  - **Out of scope:** Shared infrastructure, administrative support, appointment history, technical capability, advice, professional association, or ordinary coordination does not by itself establish a Material Control Line. The relationship must carry practical power material to independent judgment or action on the particular act.
<a id="material-control-line-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) and [Oversight measurement family](subcore_03.055_apex_oversight_leg.md#measuring-oversight-preamble-measurement-family) — used together to test whether formally separate seats are functionally independent in practice.

    **Primary assessment:** Map actual power for the particular act rather than relying on titles, legal entities, reporting charts, or nominal recusal. Identify who can exercise the powers listed above, including through indirect, emergency, economic, informational, or technical leverage. Assess the combined practical effect where no single lever is conclusive by itself.
  - **Secondary measure:** [Materiality](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#materiality), [Dependency](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#dependency), [System Capture](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#system-capture), [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability), and [Attribution Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#attribution-integrity) — co-measures that reveal control hidden by formal separation or dispersed across several actors.

    **Secondary assessment:** Distinguish ordinary coordination from practical control. Ask whether the seat can access and preserve evidence, apply the governing standard, decline or condition the act, route a challenge, and seek correction without permission, suppression, or retaliation by an interested party.
<a id="material-control-line-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** Where a governing rule requires independence, a seat is non-compliant if it sits within the Material Control Line of the initiating seat or a materially interested party. Indirect control through an affiliate, contractor, committee, proxy, technical administrator, or automated system does not resolve the failure.
  - **Secondary failure:** It is non-compliant to treat a seat as independent solely because it has a different title, office, employer, legal entity, signature, or nominal recusal while material practical control remains. It is also non-compliant to assert a Material Control Line from shared infrastructure, administrative support, appointment history, or another relationship alone without showing practical power material to the act.

<a id="interested-party"></a>

#### Interested Party

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream:
  - [Chapter Six §4 Independence, Conflict, and Control Lines](core_06.000_functional_independence_separation_of_duties.md#4-independence-conflict-and-control-lines) (6.000 §4);
  - [Article XIII-B](core_04.010_rights_part_b.md#article-xiii-b-influence-contact-and-lobbying-boundaries) (*Influence-Contact and Lobbying Boundaries*) for influence-contact boundaries, and [Article XIII-C](core_04.010_rights_part_b.md#article-xiii-c-conflicts-of-interest-gifts-and-benefits) (*Conflicts of Interest, Gifts, and Benefits*) for post-service and prospective-role conflicts;
  - [Chapter Twenty-One §3.2.4](core_21.000_governance.md#324-selection-funding-boundaries) (27.000 §3.2.4) (*Selection-Funding Boundaries*) for selection-funding boundaries;
  - [Subcore 10-B §7 Record Custody and Opening Authority](subcore_10.020_standing_assessment.md#7-record-custody-and-opening-authority) (S10-B §7);
  - [Subcore 21-E §3 Role Criteria, Appointment Standards, and Removal Pathways](subcore_21.050_role_holders_interpretive_bodies.md#3-role-criteria-appointment-standards-and-removal-pathways);
  - **CI-6.210** (*Conflict integrity, anti-capture, and anti-corruption*);
  - **CJS-3.110 §6** (*integrity assurance and resilience operations*); and
  - **CF-16.425** (*Integrity safeguards, anti-capture operations, and anti-self-judging support*).
- Cluster component: [Governance Architecture, Decentralization, and Concentration](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#governance-architecture-decentralization-and-concentration).
- Read with:
  - [Material Control Line](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#material-control-line),
  - [Initiating Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#initiating-seat),
  - [Governance](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#governance),
  - [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability),
  - [System Capture](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#system-capture),
  - [Anti-Capture](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#anti-capture),
  - [Materiality](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#materiality), and
  - [Dependency](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#dependency).
- Layer: applies within both the **Constitutional Contract Layer (CCL)** and **Stakeholder System Participation (SSP)** under [Preamble §3.2](core_00.000_preamble.md#32-governance-layers).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: an interested party is anyone with a real stake in how a particular matter comes out, and anyone acting for them or under their control. The label does not take away anyone's right to be heard. It means contact with the sentients deciding the matter is made on the official record, and an interested party cannot also be the one who checks, records, or decides it.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** For a particular matter, a sentient, institution, or system that has a [material stake](core_00.000_preamble.md#material-stake) in the outcome, together with anyone, or any system, acting for it or inside its [Material Control Line](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#material-control-line). A matter is any decision, finding, certification, appointment, selection, procurement, audit, review, interpretation, or challenge. A material stake exists where the outcome could materially change the party's rights, duties, status, standing, resources, or position, whether directly or through ownership, funding, employment, contract, or comparable [dependency](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#dependency). Depending on the matter, interested parties include:
    - the initiator, requester, claimant, subject, or challenger;
    - a bidder, counterparty, funder, or beneficiary; and
    - an affected sentient whose rights or position the outcome materially changes.

    Interested-party status is decided matter by matter. A sentient can be interested in one matter and not in another.
  - **Out of scope:** A general interest in the outcome shared with the public, expertise, advice, advocacy for a cause with no stake of the advocate's own, professional association, or ordinary participation does not by itself make a sentient an interested party. The stake must be material to the particular matter. Interested-party status never removes a right to participate, be heard, or contest. It governs how the party may contact the sentients deciding the matter and which seats it may hold. Those rules are stated in [Chapter Six](core_06.000_functional_independence_separation_of_duties.md#chapter-six-functional-independence-and-separation-of-duties), [Article XIII-B](core_04.010_rights_part_b.md#article-xiii-b-influence-contact-and-lobbying-boundaries) (*Influence-Contact and Lobbying Boundaries*), and the implementation text listed above; this entry is the Chapter Three meaning only.
  - **Depends on:** [Materiality](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#materiality); [Dependency](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#dependency); [Material Control Line](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#material-control-line).
<a id="interested-party-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) and [Oversight measurement family](subcore_03.055_apex_oversight_leg.md#measuring-oversight-preamble-measurement-family) — used together to test whether a body identified who has a material stake in a matter and kept those parties out of the checking seats.

    **Primary assessment:** Evaluate whether:
    1. the interested parties were identified for the matter from the actual stake, and not from titles, legal entities, intermediaries, or who happened to appear;
    2. no interested party, and no one in an interested party's Material Control Line, held the verify-or-authorize, record, or contest seat for the matter; and
    3. contact between interested parties and those deciding or supporting the matter was recorded and disclosed under the applicable rules, with open advocacy on the official record left available.
<a id="interested-party-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** Treat a party with a material stake as not interested because of its title, legal entity, intermediary, nominal arm's length, or because it did not appear in the matter. Indirect interest through an affiliate, funder, contractor, proxy, or automated system does not resolve the failure.
  - **Secondary failure:** Use interested-party status as a reason to keep an affected sentient from being heard, or assert the status from a general public interest, advice, or shared infrastructure alone without showing a material stake in the particular matter.

<a id="independence"></a>
<a id="independent"></a>

#### Independence

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream:
  - [Chapter Six §4 Independence, Conflict, and Control Lines](core_06.000_functional_independence_separation_of_duties.md#4-independence-conflict-and-control-lines) (6.000 §4);
  - [Subcore 10-B §7 Record Custody and Opening Authority](subcore_10.020_standing_assessment.md#7-record-custody-and-opening-authority) (S10-B §7); Chapter Twenty-Two forum anti-self-judging;
  - **CI-6.205 §2** (*Functional Separation Lanes*);
  - **CI-21.205 §6** (*Seat catalog*);
  - **CI-6.210** (*Conflict, integrity, anti-capture, and anti-corruption*); and
  - **CF-16.425** (*Integrity safeguards, anti-capture operations, and anti-self-judging support*).
- Cluster component: [Governance Architecture, Decentralization, and Concentration](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#governance-architecture-decentralization-and-concentration).
- Read with:
  - [Material Control Line](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#material-control-line),
  - [Interested Party](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#interested-party),
  - [Verify-or-Authorize Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#verify-or-authorize-seat),
  - [Record Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#record-seat),
  - [Contest Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contest-seat),
  - [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability), and
  - [Verifiability](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#verifiability) (independence from operator self-assertion).
- Layer: applies within both the **Constitutional Contract Layer (CCL)** and **Stakeholder System Participation (SSP)** under [Preamble §3.2](core_00.000_preamble.md#32-governance-layers).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: a seat, body, or system is independent of an act when the party seeking the result cannot control it on that act and it has no stake of its own in how the act comes out. A different title, signature, or company does not make a seat, body, or system independent.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** For a particular act or matter, a sentient, office, body, or system is **independent** of the party whose act is at issue when both hold:
    - it is outside the [Material Control Line](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#material-control-line) of that party and of every [Interested Party](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#interested-party) in the matter; and
    - it is not itself an Interested Party in the matter.

    Independence is judged act by act, on actual power and stake, not on titles, legal entities, signatures, or nominal recusal. A sentient can be independent on one act and not on another. An independent check is also one that can be carried out: the holder has authority to apply the governing standard, access to the evidence, and the practical ability to decline or condition the act and to route a challenge.
  - **Out of scope:**
    - Expertise, advice, professional association, shared infrastructure, administrative support, appointment history, or ordinary coordination does not by itself defeat independence. Practical control or a material stake in the particular act does.
    - Independence is not indifference. An independent holder may hold views, work in the same field, or belong to the same community.
    - Which seats exist, who may hold them, and how a map places them are Chapter Six questions, not part of this definition.
    - "Independent term" and "semi-independent term" in this chapter are labels for how a definition stands. They do not use this meaning.
  - **Depends on:** [Material Control Line](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#material-control-line); [Interested Party](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#interested-party).
<a id="independence-a"></a>
<a id="independent-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) and [Oversight measurement family](subcore_03.055_apex_oversight_leg.md#measuring-oversight-preamble-measurement-family) — used together to test whether a holder is independent of the act it checks.

    **Primary assessment:** For the particular act, evaluate whether:
    1. the holder sits outside the Material Control Line of the party whose act is at issue and of each interested party;
    2. the holder is not itself an interested party in the matter; and
    3. the holder has the authority, evidence access, and practical ability to decline, condition, and route.
<a id="independence-c"></a>
<a id="independent-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** Treat a seat, body, system, or tool as independent because of a different title, office, employer, legal entity, signature, committee, internal label, nominal recusal, or tool-generated attestation, when it sits inside the Material Control Line of the party whose act is at issue or is itself an interested party. A second signature does not resolve the failure.
  - **Secondary failure:** Assert that a holder is not independent from expertise, advice, professional association, shared infrastructure, or appointment history alone, without showing practical control or a material stake in the particular act.

#### Initiating Seat

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream:
