  - [Chapter Six §4 Independence, conflict, and control lines](core_06.000_functional_independence_separation_of_duties.md#4-independence-conflict-and-control-lines) (6.000 §4);
  - [Subcore 10-B §7 Record Custody and Opening Authority](subcore_10.020_standing_assessment.md#7-record-custody-and-opening-authority) (S10-B §7); and
  - **CI-21.205 §6** (*Seat Catalog — Process-Role Archetypes and Operational Boundaries*).
- Cluster component: [Governance Architecture, Decentralization, and Concentration](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#governance-architecture-decentralization-and-concentration).
- Read with:
  - [Material Control Line](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#material-control-line),
  - [Verify-or-Authorize Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#verify-or-authorize-seat),
  - [Record Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#record-seat),
  - [Contest Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contest-seat),
  - [Accountability](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#accountability),
  - [Oversight](subcore_03.055_apex_oversight_leg.md#oversight-constitutional), and
  - [Attribution Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#attribution-integrity).
- Layer: applies within both the **Constitutional Contract Layer (CCL)** and **Stakeholder System Participation (SSP)** under [Preamble §3.2](core_00.000_preamble.md#32-governance-layers).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: the Initiating Seat is the role that starts a particular act. It belongs to the sentient, office, or system seeking, proposing, operating, claiming, or otherwise setting that act in motion—even when a tool, agent, or intermediary transmits the request.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** The defined responsibility and authority, for a particular act, to request, propose, operate, claim, or otherwise begin the act. The seat follows the real source of the objective, claim, or operation through agents, automation, and other intermediaries.
  - **Out of scope:** Neutral intake, transmission, scheduling, or routing that neither sponsors the requested outcome nor operates or claims the act does not by itself place the carrier in the Initiating Seat.
<a id="initiating-seat-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) and [Oversight measurement family](subcore_03.055_apex_oversight_leg.md#measuring-oversight-preamble-measurement-family) — used together to identify who actually began the act and whether the required independent check is real.

    **Primary assessment:** Trace the objective, claim, instruction, operation, and requested outcome to their actual source. Look through nominal applicants, agents, automated interfaces, contractors, and other intermediaries. Distinguish the party seeking or operating the act from a neutral channel that merely receives or routes it.
  - **Secondary measure:** [Attribution Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#attribution-integrity), [Material Control Line](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#material-control-line), and [Verifiability](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#verifiability) — co-measures for hidden initiation, proxying, and reconstructability.

    **Secondary assessment:** Check whether the Act Record identifies who supplied the material objective, evidence, claim, or operating decision and whether proxying or automation obscures that source.
<a id="initiating-seat-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** The Initiating Seat is misidentified or concealed, or it supplies the supposedly independent verification or authorization of its own act where separation is required.
  - **Secondary failure:** A tool, agent, intermediary, title, or formal organizational boundary is used to erase the actual initiator or make self-verification appear independent.

---

<a id="verify-or-authorize-seat"></a>

#### Verify-or-Authorize Seat

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream:
  - [Chapter Six §3 Four-Seat Constitutional Requirement](core_06.000_functional_independence_separation_of_duties.md#3-four-seat-constitutional-requirement) (6.000 §3) (*operative separation and incompatibility rules*);
  - [Chapter Six §4 Independence, conflict, and control lines](core_06.000_functional_independence_separation_of_duties.md#4-independence-conflict-and-control-lines) (6.000 §4);
  - [Subcore 10-B §7 Record Custody and Opening Authority](subcore_10.020_standing_assessment.md#7-record-custody-and-opening-authority) (S10-B §7); and
  - **CI-21.205 §6** (*Seat Catalog — Process-Role Archetypes and Operational Boundaries*).
- Cluster component: [Governance Architecture, Decentralization, and Concentration](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#governance-architecture-decentralization-and-concentration).
- Read with:
  - [Initiating Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#initiating-seat),
  - [Record Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#record-seat),
  - [Contest Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contest-seat),
  - [Material Control Line](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#material-control-line),
  - [Evidence Preservation](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#evidence-preservation),
  - [Verifiability](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#verifiability), and
  - [Accountability](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#accountability).
- Layer: applies within both the **Constitutional Contract Layer (CCL)** and **Stakeholder System Participation (SSP)** under [Preamble §3.2](core_00.000_preamble.md#32-governance-layers).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: the Verify-or-Authorize Seat is the role that independently checks the evidence and authority for a particular act against the governing standard, then verifies, authorizes, declines, or conditions the act. A signature, checklist, or automated test is not this seat unless it carries that real checking and decision authority.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** The defined responsibility and authority, for a particular act, to examine the evidence and legal or constitutional authority against the governing standard and to verify, authorize, decline, or condition the act.
  - **Out of scope:** Advice, intake screening, technical testing, a checklist, a countersignature, or an automated result that cannot independently apply the governing standard and decline, condition, or route the act.
<a id="verify-or-authorize-seat-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Oversight measurement family](subcore_03.055_apex_oversight_leg.md#measuring-oversight-preamble-measurement-family) and [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) — used together to test the quality, authority, and independence of the verification.

    **Primary assessment:** Identify the governing standard, evidence available, decision actually made, and holder's authority to decline or condition the act. Test whether the holder is outside the [Material Control Line](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#material-control-line) of the Initiating Seat and any materially interested party.
  - **Secondary measure:** [Verifiability](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#verifiability), [Evidence Preservation](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#evidence-preservation), [Necessity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#necessity), and [Attribution Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#attribution-integrity) — co-measures for evidentiary sufficiency, decision quality, and reconstructability.

    **Secondary assessment:** Check whether the determination identifies the evidence, authority, standard, conditions, reasons for decline, and route for unresolved issues rather than recording only an approval label.
<a id="verify-or-authorize-seat-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** The holder lacks the evidence, governing standard, authority, or practical independence needed to make the determination, or verifies or authorizes an act initiated through its own [Material Control Line](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#material-control-line).
  - **Secondary failure:** A nominal reviewer, signature, checklist, model output, or technical test is treated as independent verification even though it cannot meaningfully examine, decline, condition, or route the act.

---

<a id="record-opening-authority"></a>

#### Record-Opening Authority

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream:
  - [Subcore 10-B §7 Record Custody and Opening Authority](subcore_10.020_standing_assessment.md#7-record-custody-and-opening-authority) (S10-B §7);
  - [Chapter Five §6 The Charter Role Map and Standing-Record Custody](core_05.000_charters.md#6-the-charter-role-map-and-standing-record-custody) (5.000 §6);
  - [Chapter Six §3 Four-Seat Constitutional Requirement](core_06.000_functional_independence_separation_of_duties.md#3-four-seat-constitutional-requirement) (6.000 §3);
  - **CI-6.205 §6** (*System and Institutional Charter Contents, Review, and Formation Template*); and
  - **CF-16.415 §9** (*Backup Record Authority*).
- Cluster component: [Governance Architecture, Decentralization, and Concentration](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#governance-architecture-decentralization-and-concentration).
- Read with:
  - [Standing Record](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#standing-record),
  - [Verify-or-Authorize Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#verify-or-authorize-seat),
  - [Record Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#record-seat),
  - [Record Custodian](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#record-custodian),
  - [Contest Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contest-seat),
  - [Material Control Line](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#material-control-line),
  - [Charter Role Map](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#charter-role-map), and
  - [Evidence Preservation](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#evidence-preservation).
- Layer: applies within both the **Constitutional Contract Layer (CCL)** and **Stakeholder System Participation (SSP)** under [Preamble §3.2](core_00.000_preamble.md#32-governance-layers).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: the Record-Opening Authority is the body that checks the facts and decides whether a Standing Record may be opened, corrected, ended, or replaced. It holds the Verify-or-Authorize Seat for that official record. It does not keep the official record, which is the Record Custodian's job, and it does not hear a challenge to the official record, which is the Contest Seat's job. It cannot be the official record's subject, the party claiming or accusing, the challenger, or anyone who controls or is controlled by them. If a Chartered Party's own map names none, or cannot fill the seat, the Backup Record Authority does the job, so nobody's record waits on the Chartered Party.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** The body that holds the [Verify-or-Authorize Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#verify-or-authorize-seat) for a [Standing Record](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#standing-record). It is named and its authority published before it acts. It verifies the factual basis of an opening, correction, expiry, or supersession and authorizes it, and it names itself on the official record with its reason. It may be an office or role placed by a [Charter Role Map](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#charter-role-map), a forum acting as a record-opening authority, a body that relies on the official record but has no stake in how it comes out and no dealing with the subject, a claimant, or a challenger that gives them practical power over it, or the Backup Record Authority (**CF-16.415 §9** (*Backup Record Authority*)), which is the default where a System Charter or Institutional Charter names none or has a gap.
  - **Out of scope:** The Record Custodian that enters and holds the official record. The Contest Seat that hears a challenge. A registrar that files and receipts. A body that only supplies inputs such as a recorder's statement, a log, or a photograph. A forum's merits decision. Any body that only requests the official record.
<a id="record-opening-authority-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Oversight measurement family](subcore_03.055_apex_oversight_leg.md#measuring-oversight-preamble-measurement-family) and [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) — used together to test whether each change to a Standing Record was authorized by a named body that is independent of the official record's subject, claimant, and challenger.

    **Primary assessment:** Identify who authorized each opening, correction, expiry, or supersession. Check that the authority was named before it acted, that it sits outside the [Material Control Line](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#material-control-line) of the subject, the claimant, and the challenger, that the facts were verified to the standard the governing process sets, and that it gave reasons.
  - **Secondary measure:** [Auditability](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#auditability), [Attribution Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#attribution-integrity), [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability), and [Evidence Preservation](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#evidence-preservation) — co-measures for traceability and correction.

    **Secondary assessment:** Test whether the official record names the authority, its reason, and the version of the System Charter or Institutional Charter in force; whether an authority with an interest in the official record declined and routed it; and whether delay or refusal by the authority was treated as a defect to log and correct, not as a gate on anyone's right to form or operate.
<a id="record-opening-authority-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** The official record's subject, a claimant, a challenger, or anyone in their Material Control Line acts as the authority; the authority also enters and holds the official record or hears the contest of an official record it authorized; or it authorizes a change without verifying the facts.
  - **Secondary failure:** The authority is not named before it acts, or is named only by the party it checks; the official record omits the authority, its reason, or the version of the System Charter or Institutional Charter in force; a conflicted authority does not decline and route; or the Chartered Party chooses, appoints, or funds a backup authority through a channel it controls.

---

<a id="record-seat"></a>

#### Record Seat

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream:
  - [Chapter Six §3 Four-Seat Constitutional Requirement](core_06.000_functional_independence_separation_of_duties.md#3-four-seat-constitutional-requirement) (6.000 §3) (*operative separation and incompatibility rules*);
  - [Chapter Six §4 Independence, conflict, and control lines](core_06.000_functional_independence_separation_of_duties.md#4-independence-conflict-and-control-lines) (6.000 §4);
  - [Subcore 10-B §7 Record Custody and Opening Authority](subcore_10.020_standing_assessment.md#7-record-custody-and-opening-authority) (S10-B §7); and
  - **CI-21.205 §6** (*Seat Catalog — Process-Role Archetypes and Operational Boundaries*).
- Cluster component: [Governance Architecture, Decentralization, and Concentration](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#governance-architecture-decentralization-and-concentration).
- Read with:
  - [Initiating Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#initiating-seat),
  - [Verify-or-Authorize Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#verify-or-authorize-seat),
  - [Contest Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contest-seat),
  - [Evidence Preservation](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#evidence-preservation),
  - [Attribution Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#attribution-integrity), and
  - [Auditability](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#auditability).
- Layer: applies within both the **Constitutional Contract Layer (CCL)** and **Stakeholder System Participation (SSP)** under [Preamble §3.2](core_00.000_preamble.md#32-governance-layers).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: the Record Seat is the role that makes and keeps the official record of what the Verify-or-Authorize Seat determined. It records the determination; it does not create or independently verify that determination.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** The defined responsibility and authority, for a particular act, to enter, version, hold, preserve, and publish the official record of what the Verify-or-Authorize Seat determined, together with the attribution and audit trail the governing standard requires.
  - **Out of scope:** Incidental storage, automated logging, clerical transmission, or possession of a copy without authority and responsibility for the official record. The seat does not include authority to verify the facts or substitute its own determination.
<a id="record-seat-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) and [Oversight measurement family](subcore_03.055_apex_oversight_leg.md#measuring-oversight-preamble-measurement-family) — used together to test whether an official determination remains attributable, intact, accessible, and independently recorded.

    **Primary assessment:** Identify who has authority to enter and alter the official record. Check attribution, version history, preservation, publication where required, challenge status, and the link to the Verify-or-Authorize Seat's actual determination.
  - **Secondary measure:** [Evidence Preservation](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#evidence-preservation), [Attribution Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#attribution-integrity), [Auditability](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#auditability), and [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability) — co-measures for integrity, reconstruction, and correction.

    **Secondary assessment:** Test whether the official record can be reconstructed without treating the recorder's statement as proof of the underlying merits, and whether a challenge can be marked and routed without alteration or suppression.
<a id="record-seat-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** The Record Seat creates, changes, or enters a determination on its own judgment; records an unverified claim as an authorized determination; or is held by the Verify-or-Authorize Seat for the same act where separation is required.
  - **Secondary failure:** The official record lacks attribution, version integrity, preservation, required publication, challenge status, or a reconstructable link to the determination it records.

---

<a id="record-custodian"></a>

#### Record Custodian

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream:
  - [Subcore 10-B §7 Record Custody and Opening Authority](subcore_10.020_standing_assessment.md#7-record-custody-and-opening-authority) (S10-B §7);
  - [Chapter Five §6 The Charter Role Map and Standing-Record Custody](core_05.000_charters.md#6-the-charter-role-map-and-standing-record-custody) (5.000 §6);
  - **CI-6.205 §6** (*System and Institutional Charter Contents, Review, and Formation Template*); and
  - **CF-16.415 §11** (*Backup Record Custodian*).
- Cluster component: [Governance Architecture, Decentralization, and Concentration](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#governance-architecture-decentralization-and-concentration).
- Read with:
  - [Record Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#record-seat),
  - [Record-Opening Authority](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#record-opening-authority),
  - [Standing Record](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#standing-record),
  - [Contest Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contest-seat),
  - [Material Control Line](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#material-control-line),
  - [Charter Role Map](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#charter-role-map), and
  - [Evidence Preservation](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#evidence-preservation).
- Layer: applies within both the **Constitutional Contract Layer (CCL)** and **Stakeholder System Participation (SSP)** under [Preamble §3.2](core_00.000_preamble.md#32-governance-layers).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: the Record Custodian is the body that enters, keeps, and amends a Standing Record after an authority has authorized it. It keeps what was verified. It does not verify, and it does not hear a challenge. It cannot be the official record's subject, a claimant, a challenger, or anyone who controls or is controlled by them.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** The body that holds the [Record Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#record-seat) for a [Standing Record](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#standing-record): it enters each authorized version, holds it, controls versioning, publishes the challenge path, and answers for the official record's continuity. It may be placed by a [Charter Role Map](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#charter-role-map), be a substitute holder for one official record, or be the Backup Record Custodian (**CF-16.415 §11** (*Backup Record Custodian*)).
  - **Out of scope:** The [Record-Opening Authority](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#record-opening-authority) that verifies and authorizes. The Contest Seat. A registrar that files and receipts. Incidental storage or a copy held without authority over the official record.
<a id="record-custodian-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) and [Oversight measurement family](subcore_03.055_apex_oversight_leg.md#measuring-oversight-preamble-measurement-family) — used together to test whether each Standing Record is held by a named body that is independent of its subject, claimants, and challengers, with the full audit trail.

    **Primary assessment:** Identify who holds each official record and each version. Check that the custodian was named before it acted, sits outside the [Material Control Line](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#material-control-line) of the subject, claimant, and challenger, is not the authority for the same official record, and keeps version history and the published challenge path.
  - **Secondary measure:** [Evidence Preservation](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#evidence-preservation), [Attribution Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#attribution-integrity), [Auditability](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#auditability), and [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability) — co-measures for integrity, reconstruction, and correction.

    **Secondary assessment:** Test whether a challenge was logged on receipt, whether custody passed with its full trail when a conflict or gap arose, and whether the custodian applied no effect and withheld no path.
<a id="record-custodian-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** The custodian is the subject, a claimant, a challenger, or in their Material Control Line; it also verifies or hears the contest of the same official record; or it enters a version no authority authorized.
  - **Secondary failure:** Custody lapses, a challenge is not logged on receipt, the audit trail is lost in a transfer, the custodian conditions entry on the subject's cooperation, or the Chartered Party chooses, appoints, or funds a backup custodian through a channel it controls.

---

<a id="contest-seat"></a>

#### Contest Seat

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream:
  - [Chapter Six §3 Four-Seat Constitutional Requirement](core_06.000_functional_independence_separation_of_duties.md#3-four-seat-constitutional-requirement) (6.000 §3) (*operative separation and incompatibility rules*);
  - [Chapter Six §4 Independence, conflict, and control lines](core_06.000_functional_independence_separation_of_duties.md#4-independence-conflict-and-control-lines) (6.000 §4);
  - [Subcore 10-B §7 Record Custody and Opening Authority](subcore_10.020_standing_assessment.md#7-record-custody-and-opening-authority) (S10-B §7);
  - [Chapter Sixteen Forums and Jurisdiction](core_16.000_forum.md#chapter-sixteen-standards-routing-and-forum-cases) (22.000); and
  - **CI-21.205 §6** (*Seat Catalog — Process-Role Archetypes and Operational Boundaries*).
- Cluster component: [Governance Architecture, Decentralization, and Concentration](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#governance-architecture-decentralization-and-concentration).
- Read with:
  - [Initiating Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#initiating-seat),
  - [Verify-or-Authorize Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#verify-or-authorize-seat),
  - [Record Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#record-seat),
  - [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability),
  - [Due Process](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#due-process),
  - [Timely Resolution](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#timely-resolution), and
  - [Material Control Line](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#material-control-line).
- Layer: applies within both the **Constitutional Contract Layer (CCL)** and **Stakeholder System Participation (SSP)** under [Preamble §3.2](core_00.000_preamble.md#32-governance-layers).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: the Contest Seat is the role that receives and reviews a challenge to a particular act or its Act Record, orders correction or limits where authorized, and sends any issue it cannot decide to the proper independent path. Receiving a complaint is not enough if the recipient cannot review or route it.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** The defined responsibility and authority, for a particular act, to receive and review a challenge, order correction or limits where authorized, and route any issue outside its authority to the proper forum or independent path.
  - **Out of scope:** A general help desk, intake channel, or complaint log that cannot review, preserve, mark, correct, limit, or route the challenged act. The seat does not gain authority over issues assigned to another forum or decision-maker.
<a id="contest-seat-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) and [Timeliness measurement family](subcore_03.065_apex_timeliness_leg.md#measuring-timeliness) — used together to test whether challenge produces independent review, correction, limits, or routing inside the **intake target** of the matching tier ([CF-16.445 §5.1](corpus_forum/cf_16.445_performance_backlog_publication_accessibility.md#cf-16445-51-stage-targets-and-outer-bounds) (*Stage targets and outer bounds*)) rather than a procedural dead end.

    **Primary assessment:** Identify the published challenge path, holder, authority, clock, available corrective or limiting action, and required routing. Test whether the holder is independent of the challenged verification and outside the [Material Control Line](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#material-control-line) of any materially interested party.
  - **Secondary measure:** [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability), [Due Process](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#due-process), [Timely Resolution](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#timely-resolution), [Evidence Preservation](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#evidence-preservation), and [Attribution Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#attribution-integrity) — co-measures for access, fairness, evidence integrity, and reconstructability.

    **Secondary assessment:** Check whether a challenge is received and preserved as made, the official record is marked when required, interim limits are available where authorized, and questions outside the seat's authority are routed without resetting or hiding the challenge.
<a id="contest-seat-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** The Contest Seat is unavailable, controlled by a materially interested party, held by the seat that verified or authorized the challenged act, or unable to order any authorized correction or limit or route issues beyond its authority.
  - **Secondary failure:** A nominal complaint path receives but does not preserve, review, act on, or route the challenge, or delay is used to make the challenge practically ineffective.

---

<a id="materially-binding-act"></a>

#### Materially Binding Act

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream:
  - [Chapter One §7 System and Institutional Charters, Functional Independence, and Separation of Duties](core_01.000_constitutional_map.md#7-system-and-institutional-charters-functional-independence-and-separation-of-duties);
  - [Chapter Two §18.3 Separation of Duties](core_02.050_governance_incentive_application.md#183-separation-of-duties) (2.050 §18.3);
  - [Chapter Six §1 Purpose, Role, and Scope](core_06.000_functional_independence_separation_of_duties.md#1-purpose-role-and-scope) (6.000 §1) (*application list*) through
  - [§10 Relationship to Later Processes](core_06.000_functional_independence_separation_of_duties.md#10-relationship-to-later-processes); Chapters Eight through Twenty-Seven process-specific acts; and
  - **CI-21.205 §6** (*seat catalog*).
- Cluster component: [Governance Architecture, Decentralization, and Concentration](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#governance-architecture-decentralization-and-concentration).
- Read with:
  - [Domain-Specific Materiality](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#domain-specific-materiality),
  - [Materially Binding Act Record](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#materially-binding-act-record),
  - [Attributable Action](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#attributable-action),
  - [Material](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#material),
  - [Materiality](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#materiality),
  - [Initiating Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#initiating-seat),
  - [Verify-or-Authorize Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#verify-or-authorize-seat),
  - [Record Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#record-seat),
  - [Contest Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contest-seat), and
  - [Material Control Line](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#material-control-line).
- Layer: applies within both the **Constitutional Contract Layer (CCL)** and **Stakeholder System Participation (SSP)** under [Preamble §3.2](core_00.000_preamble.md#32-governance-layers).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: a materially binding act is an official step that settles something that matters — a decision, approval, finding, record change, release, payment, or ruling on a challenge — so that others must follow it or may rely on it. Because it binds, starting it, checking it, recording it, and hearing a challenge to it must stay in separate hands. Advice, drafts, and raw logs are not binding acts; the step that adopts them or gives them effect is.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** An attributable act, taken or given effect under constitutional, adopted, or delegated authority, that creates, changes, confirms, suspends, or ends a right, duty, status, standing, permission, allocation, record, or operating state of a sentient, system, institution, or shared resource, where that effect is [Material](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#material) in at least one domain under [Domain-Specific Materiality](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#domain-specific-materiality) and others must follow it or may rely on it as settled until it is corrected, superseded, or overturned. Covered acts include those listed in [Chapter Six §1 Purpose, Role, and Scope](core_06.000_functional_independence_separation_of_duties.md#1-purpose-role-and-scope): a decision; an authorization or certification; a finding; an official record entry or material version change; a release, deployment, continuation, or withdrawal; a disbursement or allocation; and a disposition of a challenge.
  - How strong the duties on a qualifying act are still follows [Materiality](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#materiality). An act is materially binding because of what it does, not what it is called or who performs it. The same test applies whether a sentient, an office, an AI system, or an automated process takes the act, and whether it is taken under ordinary or emergency procedure. An output labeled advisory, provisional, or automated is a materially binding act when it is given effect without a separate adopting act, or when others must, or in practice do, act on it as settled.
  - Where one process step produces several materially binding acts, each is a separate act for seat placement and [Act Record](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#materially-binding-act-record) purposes. Where several steps together make one act, the act is the step that fixes its effect.
  - **Out of scope:** Deliberation, drafts, proposals, recommendations, analyses, and advisory opinions that bind no one until separately adopted (the adopting act is the materially binding act); raw telemetry, logs, attestations, and other evidence inputs; clerical steps that carry out an already recorded act without changing its effect; and acts whose effect is not material in any domain under [Domain-Specific Materiality](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#domain-specific-materiality). Conduct that materially affects sentients or shared systems without being an official act — such as the ongoing operation of a system, product, or service — is governed by [Attributable Action](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#attributable-action) and the substantive rule that applies to it, not narrowed by this definition. The act that authorizes, deploys, continues, or withdraws that operation is a materially binding act.
<a id="materially-binding-act-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) — used to test whether every act that meets this definition is identified as one, placed under the four seats, and carried by an Act Record.

    **Primary assessment:** For each process, identify every step that creates, changes, confirms, suspends, or ends a right, duty, status, standing, permission, allocation, record, or operating state. Test its effect under [Domain-Specific Materiality](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#domain-specific-materiality). Check that each qualifying step is named as a materially binding act on the published placement map required by [Chapter Six §6 Published Placement, Vacancy, and Substitution](core_06.000_functional_independence_separation_of_duties.md#6-published-placement-vacancy-and-substitution) and has an identifiable [Act Record](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#materially-binding-act-record).
  - **Secondary measure:** [Oversight measurement family](subcore_03.055_apex_oversight_leg.md#measuring-oversight-preamble-measurement-family), [Materiality](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#materiality), and [Attributable Action](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#attributable-action) — co-measures for materiality, label-based evasion, and the boundary between official acts and ongoing conduct.

    **Secondary assessment:** Sample outputs described as advisory, provisional, automated, or clerical and test whether they are in practice given effect without a separate adopting act. Check that splitting or bundling process steps does not leave the step that fixes an effect without distinct seats or an Act Record.
<a id="materially-binding-act-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** An act that meets this definition is not treated as materially binding, so it proceeds without distinct seats or an Act Record, because it is labeled advisory, provisional, automated, clerical, or emergency, or because an AI system, delegate, or automated process performed it.
  - **Secondary failure:** Process steps are split or bundled so that the step that fixes an effect cannot be identified; or the definition is stretched to treat drafts, evidence inputs, or non-material steps as materially binding acts in a way that buries the real acts in procedure.

---

<a id="act-record"></a>
<a id="materially-binding-act-record"></a>

#### Materially Binding Act Record

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream:
  - [Chapter Six §3 Four-Seat Constitutional Requirement](core_06.000_functional_independence_separation_of_duties.md#3-four-seat-constitutional-requirement) (6.000 §3) and
  - [§5 Wrong-Seat Routing](core_06.000_functional_independence_separation_of_duties.md#5-wrong-seat-routing) and
  - [§9 Act Records and Attributable Handoffs](core_06.000_functional_independence_separation_of_duties.md#9-act-records-and-attributable-handoffs) (*universal minimum contents and routing requirements*); Chapters Eight through Twenty-Seven process-specific records;
  - **CI-21.205 §6** (*seat catalog*); and
  - [`materially_binding_act_record.schema.json`](implementation/schemas/materially_binding_act_record.schema.json) (*base machine-checkable form; process support, not a second definition*).
- Cluster component: [Governance Architecture, Decentralization, and Concentration](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#governance-architecture-decentralization-and-concentration).
- Read with:
  - [Materially Binding Act](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#materially-binding-act),
  - [Attributable Action](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#attributable-action),
  - [Attribution Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#attribution-integrity),
  - [Initiating Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#initiating-seat),
  - [Verify-or-Authorize Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#verify-or-authorize-seat),
  - [Record Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#record-seat),
  - [Contest Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contest-seat),
  - [Record Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#record-integrity),
  - [Evidence Preservation](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#evidence-preservation),
  - [Auditability](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#auditability), and
  - [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability).
- Layer: applies within both the **Constitutional Contract Layer (CCL)** and **Stakeholder System Participation (SSP)** under [Preamble §3.2](core_00.000_preamble.md#32-governance-layers).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: an Act Record is the official trail for one [materially binding act](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#materially-binding-act). It shows what the act was, who held each required seat, what was checked and decided, how the matter moved, and where a challenge goes. Existing process records may carry or link this information; a second duplicate official record is not required.*

The short form for **Materially Binding Act Record** in this Constitution is **Act Record**.

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** The attributable, versioned official record for a particular [Materially Binding Act](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#materially-binding-act), maintained under the authority and responsibility of the [Record Seat](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#record-seat). It identifies and preserves the act, governing authority, required seats, determination, material handoffs and departures, clocks, challenge route and status, and the links needed to reconstruct the act under [Chapter Six §9 Act Records and Attributable Handoffs](core_06.000_functional_independence_separation_of_duties.md#9-act-records-and-attributable-handoffs).
  - An Act Record may be embodied in one applicable process-specific official record or in an attributable, integrity-preserving linked set of official records. A System Certification Record, Standing Record, Forum Case Record, or another named process record may satisfy or extend the Act Record requirements for the act it records. No duplicative standalone artifact is required where the required elements remain identifiable, linked, versioned, preserved, and accessible through the official record path.
  - **Out of scope:** Raw telemetry, a self-generated log, signature, checklist, model trace, attestation, clerical copy, unverified claim, or evidence bundle alone is not an Act Record. Those materials may be inputs or links. An Act Record does not become a [Standing Record](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#standing-record), merits determination, certification, or forum record unless the applicable process independently makes it one.
<a id="materially-binding-act-record-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) and [Oversight measurement family](subcore_03.055_apex_oversight_leg.md#measuring-oversight-preamble-measurement-family) — used together to test whether the act and its authority, seats, determination, custody, routing, and challenge posture remain reconstructable and independently reviewable.

    **Primary assessment:** Apply the minimum-content requirements in [Chapter Six §9 Act Records and Attributable Handoffs](core_06.000_functional_independence_separation_of_duties.md#9-act-records-and-attributable-handoffs). Check stable identity, attribution, version integrity, preservation, material timestamps and clocks, the governing standard and determination, each required seat and authority, every material handoff or departure, current challenge status, and links to evidence and any process-specific record that carries part of the official trail.
  - **Secondary measure:** [Attribution Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#attribution-integrity), [Evidence Preservation](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#evidence-preservation), [Auditability](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#auditability), [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability), and [Timely Resolution](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#timely-resolution) — co-measures for reliable identity, custody, reconstruction, challenge, and clocks.

    **Secondary assessment:** Test whether linked official records resolve to the same act without contradiction or silent gaps, whether superseded versions and corrections remain traceable, and whether an outsider with lawful access can follow the act from initiation through determination, record entry, challenge, and correction without treating a log or recorder statement as proof of the merits.
<a id="materially-binding-act-record-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** A materially binding act proceeds without an identifiable Act Record; the Act Record omits or obscures a required seat, authority, determination, material departure, clock, or challenge route; or a log, attestation, or unverified input is treated as the official determination.
  - **Secondary failure:** The official trail is fragmented across records that are not attributable or reliably linked; conflicting versions cannot be resolved; a correction or challenge is detached from the affected version; or a process-specific record is used to narrow the Chapter Six minimum.

<a id="decentralization"></a>

#### Decentralization

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Principles: [Chapter Two §18.6 Standardization](core_02.050_governance_incentive_application.md#186-standardization) (2.050 §18.6) (*the common-form counterweight*); [Chapter Two §9.1 Productive Capacity (Instrumental Good)](core_02.010_values_principles_continuity_aim.md#91-productive-capacity-instrumental-good) (2.010 §9.1) (non-concentration / subsidiarity read-with); [13.1.3 Proportionality](core_02.020_interaction_interpretation.md#1313-proportionality) (centralization justification).
- Cluster component: [Governance Architecture, Oversight, Dependency, Decentralization, Concentration, Market Structure, and Exit-Path Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#governance-architecture-decentralization-and-concentration).
- Read with:
  - [Governance](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#governance),
  - [Chapter Two §17.4 Aligned Self-Organization](core_02.040_stewardship_capacity_principles.md#174-aligned-self-organization) (2.040 §17.4),
  - [Oversight](subcore_03.055_apex_oversight_leg.md#oversight-constitutional),
  - [Classification-Scaled Governance](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#classification-scaled-governance),
  - [Market Concentration Threshold](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#market-concentration-threshold),
  - [Accountability](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#accountability),
  - [Necessity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#necessity), and
  - [Proportionality](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#proportionality).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: put decision-making at the smallest and least centralized level that can still meet every binding safety, truth, oversight, and system-wide requirement.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** Put decision authority at the least centralized and smallest scale that can still satisfy every binding evaluation requirement. These include:
    - [Safety (Constitutional Constraint)](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#safety-constitutional-constraint);
    - [Truth (Constitutional Constraint)](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#truth-constitutional-constraint);
    - [Material Impact](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#material-impact);
    - [Dependency](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#dependency);
    - [Classification-Scaled Governance](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#classification-scaled-governance); and
    - system-wide evaluation.
  - **Out of scope:** ordinary team task assignment or local operations detail with no decision-authority placement stake.
<a id="decentralization-a"></a>
<a id="subsidiarity-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) and [Timeliness measurement family](subcore_03.065_apex_timeliness_leg.md#measuring-timeliness) — used alongside any other relevant measures to assess responsibility and whether action happens without harmful delay.

    **Primary assessment:** Increase oversight, [Standardization](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#standardization), or central control when local capacity is not enough or effects on others are material. Do not use Decentralization to break apart [System Boundary Integrity](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#system-boundary-integrity) or evade [Accountability](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#accountability). Check who can actually decide, fund, approve, veto, reverse, appoint, or remove. Sending work or consultation downward does not decentralize authority when those powers stay above without [Necessity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#necessity).
<a id="decentralization-c"></a>
<a id="subsidiarity-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:**
    - It is non-compliant to centralize authority without [Necessity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#necessity), or to decentralize in ways that prevent required [Oversight](subcore_03.055_apex_oversight_leg.md#oversight-constitutional), defeat [Accountability](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#accountability), or produce unaddressed system-wide [Harm](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#harm).
    - Claimed decentralization is non-compliant when it shifts duties or blame downward while keeping meaningful authority, resources, information, or review access centralized without [Necessity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#necessity).
    - A Decentralization label or formal arrangement that has no real effect in the situation being evaluated is also non-compliant.

---

<a id="standardization"></a>

#### Standardization

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Principles: [Chapter Two §18.5 Modular Architecture and Dependency Discipline](core_02.050_governance_incentive_application.md#185-modular-architecture-and-dependency-discipline) (2.050 §18.5) (stable, inspectable interfaces); [Chapter Two §18.6 Standardization](core_02.050_governance_incentive_application.md#186-standardization) (2.050 §18.6); [Article XXII: Interoperability, Portability, and Exit Integrity](core_04.030_rights_part_d.md#article-xxii-interoperability-portability-and-exit-integrity).
- Read with:
  - [Decentralization](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#decentralization),
  - [Necessity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#necessity),
  - [Proportionality](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#proportionality),
  - [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability),
  - [Anti-Capture](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#anti-capture),
  - [System Capture](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#system-capture), and
  - [Meaningful Agency](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#meaningful-agency).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: when in doubt, standardize. Where no good reason supports a difference, use the common, published, interoperable way of doing it. Different treatment, formats, or procedures need a reason; sameness does not.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** The default of using one common, published, openly available form for the following, wherever no binding reason supports variation:
    - terms, definitions, and classifications;
    - interfaces, data formats, and records;
    - procedures, forms, and decision criteria; and
    - treatment of like cases and like stakeholders.
  - **Standard quality:** A standard satisfies this definition only when it is published, versioned, open to inspection and review, [contestable](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability), and implementable without a license, fee, or dependency that would give its owner control over others.
  - **Out of scope:** Uniformity of substance, ends, values, or lawful choices. This definition standardizes form and treatment. It does not standardize what stakeholders may pursue, believe, or create.
<a id="standardization-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) and [Oversight measurement family](subcore_03.055_apex_oversight_leg.md#measuring-oversight-preamble-measurement-family) — used alongside any other relevant measures to assess whether like cases are treated alike and whether the standard in use can be inspected and checked.

    **Primary assessment:** Identify each material variation in terms, interfaces, records, procedures, criteria, or treatment of like cases. For each, check whether a published common standard exists and whether the variation is supported by [Necessity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#necessity), a binding safety, truth, or rights requirement, or a documented [Proportionality](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#proportionality) reason. Where [Decentralization](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#decentralization) places authority locally, check that local choices stay interoperable with the common standard unless a documented reason says otherwise.
  - **Secondary measure:** [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability), [Anti-Capture](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#anti-capture), and [Meaningful Agency](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#meaningful-agency) — co-measures for who controls the standard, whether it can be challenged and revised, and whether it leaves real room for choice.

    **Secondary assessment:** Test who writes, owns, and amends the standard; whether a dominant actor can use it to lock in dependents or exclude competitors; whether affected stakeholders can challenge it and propose revisions; and whether exit or substitution remains workable.
<a id="standardization-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:**
    - It is non-compliant to vary terms, interfaces, procedures, criteria, or treatment of like cases without a documented reason when a published common standard is available.
    - It is non-compliant to use a private, undisclosed, or unreviewable standard in place of a published one when affected stakeholders depend on it.
    - A Standardization label for a standard that is not published, versioned, open to review, and contestable has no effect in the situation being evaluated and is non-compliant.
  - **Secondary failure:**
    - A standard that overrides a Chapter Four right, a binding safety or truth requirement, or a lawful choice that falls outside what this definition standardizes is non-compliant to that extent.
    - A standard captured by a dominant actor, or one that locks in dependents or blocks exit or substitution, is non-compliant. It must be corrected under [Anti-Capture](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#anti-capture).
    - Standardization used to centralize authority without [Necessity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#necessity), or to break apart [Decentralization](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#decentralization) where local capacity suffices, is non-compliant.

---

<a id="market-structure-constitutional"></a>

#### Market Structure

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Accountability measurement family (*measuring market structure and contestability*); Principles:
  - [Chapter Two §11 Market Structure](core_02.010_values_principles_continuity_aim.md#11-market-structure) (2.010 §11);
  - [Chapter Two §11.1 Market Concentration Threshold Mechanism (Adopter-Tunable)](core_02.010_values_principles_continuity_aim.md#111-market-concentration-threshold-mechanism-adopter-tunable) (2.010 §11.1);
  - [Chapter Two §11.2 Pro-Competition and Anti-Domination](core_02.010_values_principles_continuity_aim.md#112-pro-competition-and-anti-domination) (2.010 §11.2);
  - [Chapter Two §11.3 Consolidation Ceiling](core_02.010_values_principles_continuity_aim.md#113-consolidation-ceiling) (2.010 §11.3); **CJS-3.125 §11.2 — Anti-domination conduct and remediation catalog** (banned conduct and remedies); **CJS-3.125 §11.3 — Consolidation ceiling-setting discipline (adopter-tunable)** (how to set the ceiling).
- Cluster component: [Governance Architecture, Oversight, Dependency, Decentralization, Concentration, Market Structure, and Exit-Path Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#governance-architecture-decentralization-and-concentration); [Stewardship, Governance Discipline, and Shared-System Capacity](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#defc2-stewardship-governance-discipline-and-shared-system-capacity).
- Read with:
  - [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability),
  - [Market Concentration Threshold](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#market-concentration-threshold),
  - [Productive Capacity](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#productive-capacity),
  - [Constitutional Efficiency](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#constitutional-efficiency),
  - [Proxy Divergence](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#proxy-divergence),
  - [Dependency](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#dependency),
  - [Meaningful Agency](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#meaningful-agency),
  - [System Capture](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#system-capture), and
  - [Chapter Two §19.1.5 Constitutional Outcome Claims Discipline](core_02.050_governance_incentive_application.md#1915-constitutional-outcome-claims-discipline) (2.050 §19.1.5).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: markets and market-like systems must leave participants real choices — fair entry, workable exit, substitutes, bargaining without coercion, and reviewable access to essential infrastructure. Size or innovation alone is not a violation, but domination and dependency still require scrutiny.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** The constitutional rules for how the following **market-structuring arrangements** must preserve contestable participation:
    - markets;
    - platforms;
    - labor arrangements;
    - resource flows;
    - access to data and compute;
    - credentialing;
    - access to capital;
    - information-sphere gatekeeping; and
    - similar arrangements.

    This includes:
    - meaningful substitutes;
    - workable exit;
    - fair entry and re-entry;
    - bargaining without coercion; and
    - reviewable access to essential or high-dependency infrastructure.

    Lawful scale, integration, or a temporary advantage from genuine innovation is not prohibited by itself.
  - **Out of scope:** ordinary private exchange among peers with no market-structuring arrangement affecting contestable participation.
<a id="market-structure-constitutional-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) — *Do reward structures, market power, and answerability keep duties real?*

    **Primary assessment:** Assess real concentration, domination, and consolidation risk, not just the formal number of entities or whether a market appears open on paper.
  - **Secondary measure:** [Market Concentration Threshold](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#market-concentration-threshold), [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability), [Meaningful Agency](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#meaningful-agency), and [Dependency](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#dependency) — co-measures that can defeat contestability even when formal market openness exists.

    **Secondary assessment:** When evaluating the primary trace, apply [Market Concentration Threshold](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#market-concentration-threshold), the anti-domination rules in [Chapter Two §11.2 Pro-Competition and Anti-Domination](core_02.010_values_principles_continuity_aim.md#112-pro-competition-and-anti-domination), [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability), and [Dependency](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#dependency). Do so whenever livelihood, agency, wellbeing, ecological integrity, interoperability, exit, or constitutional review depends on the arrangement.
  - **Tertiary measure:** [Proxy Divergence](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#proxy-divergence) — where entity count, competitiveness indices, or efficiency claims substitute for substantive contestability.

    **Tertiary assessment:** When operators rely on measures of competitiveness or efficiency, apply [Proxy Divergence](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#proxy-divergence). Separate lasting Contestability from market openness that exists only on paper.
<a id="market-structure-constitutional-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** A market-structuring arrangement foreseeably degrades wellbeing, meaningful agency, dignity, ecological integrity, Contestability, or constitutional review. A market-structure label or formal arrangement that has no real effect in the situation being evaluated is also non-compliant.
  - **Secondary failure:** Concentration or domination is defended only by claims about efficiency, competitiveness, emergency, security, or productive capacity without traceable constitutional outcomes; or a threshold or ceiling regime nullifies the [Chapter Two §11 Market Structure](core_02.010_values_principles_continuity_aim.md#11-market-structure) requirements.
  - **Tertiary failure:** An arrangement passes non-concentration tests on paper but fails applicable Rights-Floor protections, or proxy satisfaction is treated as decisive when [Proxy Divergence](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#proxy-divergence) is reasonably observable.

---

<a id="market-concentration-threshold-constitutional"></a>

#### Market Concentration Threshold

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Principles:
  - [Chapter Two §9.1 Productive Capacity (Instrumental Good)](core_02.010_values_principles_continuity_aim.md#91-productive-capacity-instrumental-good) (2.010 §9.1);
  - [Chapter Two §11.1 Market Concentration Threshold Mechanism (Adopter-Tunable)](core_02.010_values_principles_continuity_aim.md#111-market-concentration-threshold-mechanism-adopter-tunable) (2.010 §11.1) (principle-layer direction); **CJS-3.125 §11.1 — Market concentration threshold-setting discipline (adopter-tunable)** (operative threshold-setting discipline);
  - [Chapter Two §11.2 Pro-Competition and Anti-Domination](core_02.010_values_principles_continuity_aim.md#112-pro-competition-and-anti-domination) (2.010 §11.2);
  - [Chapter Two §11.3 Consolidation Ceiling](core_02.010_values_principles_continuity_aim.md#113-consolidation-ceiling) (2.010 §11.3).
- Cluster component: [Governance Architecture, Oversight, Dependency, Decentralization, Concentration, Market Structure, and Exit-Path Integrity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#governance-architecture-decentralization-and-concentration).
- Read with:
  - [Productive Capacity](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#productive-capacity),
  - [Constitutional Efficiency](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#constitutional-efficiency),
  - [Proxy Divergence](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#proxy-divergence),
  - [System Boundary Integrity](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#system-boundary-integrity),
  - [Adversarial, Scaled, and Exploited Conditions](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#adversarial-scaled-and-exploited-conditions),
  - [Essential-Environment Non-Commodification](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#essential-environment-non-commodification), and
  - [Sentience Non-Exclusion](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#sentience-non-exclusion).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: adopters set the point at which too much control over a market, platform, infrastructure, labor, data, compute, capital, resources, or information triggers stronger review or remedy. They may tune that point to context, but may not set it so high that the Chapter Two §11 (*Market Structure*) protection never works.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** The operational trigger for when material concentration in a covered domain requires heightened review, intervention, or structural remedy.
    - **Covered domains:** jurisdictional authority, capability, markets, platforms, infrastructure, labor, suppliers, data, compute, credentialing, access to capital, control of resources, and the information sphere.
    - **Adopter tuning:** Adopters may tune the threshold to context.
    - **Principle-layer owner:** [Chapter Two §11.1 Market Concentration Threshold Mechanism (Adopter-Tunable)](core_02.010_values_principles_continuity_aim.md#111-market-concentration-threshold-mechanism-adopter-tunable).
    - **Controlling principles:** The threshold remains subject to the [Chapter Two §11 Market Structure](core_02.010_values_principles_continuity_aim.md#11-market-structure) non-concentration rule, the [Chapter Two §11.2 Pro-Competition and Anti-Domination](core_02.010_values_principles_continuity_aim.md#112-pro-competition-and-anti-domination) pro-competition and anti-domination discipline, and the [Chapter Two §11.3 Consolidation Ceiling](core_02.010_values_principles_continuity_aim.md#113-consolidation-ceiling) consolidation-ceiling trigger.
    - **Operational discipline:** **CJS-3.125 §11.1 — Market concentration threshold-setting discipline (adopter-tunable)**.
    - **Rights-layer pointer:** [Article V](core_04.000_rights_part_a.md#article-v-resource-allocation-dependencies-and-ecosystem-funding) (*Resource Allocation, Dependencies, and Ecosystem Funding*).
    - **Anti-nullification:** Adopter-tunable thresholds and ceilings cannot nullify the Chapter Two §11 (*Market Structure*) requirements against concentration that predictably degrades wellbeing, agency, dignity, or ecological integrity.
    - **Application:** This definition applies across substrates and jurisdictions.
  - **Out of scope:** ordinary firm size, temporary innovation lead, or lawful scale below the operational trigger for heightened review.
<a id="market-concentration-threshold-constitutional-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) — *Do reward structures, market power, and answerability keep duties real?*

    **Primary assessment:** Measure real concentration, consolidation, and lasting domination risk, not just the formal number of entities. Include indirect arrangements that produce concentrated power:
    - **Entity indirection:** federated structures and shell entities.
    - **Control and governance:** interlocking directorates and common control.
    - **Consolidation:** repeated acquisitions and cross-platform integration.
    - **Infrastructure and dependency:** control of essential infrastructure, labor markets dominated by one or a few employers, and control through supplier dependency.
    - **Gatekeeping:** capture of data or interfaces, compute chokepoints, capital-access gatekeeping, and information-sphere control.
  - **Secondary measure:** Use these co-measures:
    - [Market Structure](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#market-structure);
    - [Proxy Divergence](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#proxy-divergence);
    - [Contestability](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#contestability); and
    - [System Boundary Integrity](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#system-boundary-integrity).

    These co-measures can defeat threshold discipline even when nominal tests appear satisfied.

    **Secondary assessment:**
    - **Understated concentration:** Apply [Proxy Divergence](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#proxy-divergence) when a regime uses measures that foreseeably understate concentration.
    - **Stress conditions:** Consider [Adversarial, Scaled, and Exploited Conditions](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#adversarial-scaled-and-exploited-conditions).
    - **Heightened standards:** Apply the relevant article's higher standards regardless of the ordinary threshold when concentration materially implicates:
      - [Article I-D](core_04.000_rights_part_a.md#article-i-d-existential-risk-and-ecological-recovery-capacity) (*Existential Risk and Ecological Recovery Capacity*);
      - [Article XIV-A](core_04.020_rights_part_c.md#article-xiv-a-reliability-and-trustworthiness-baseline) (*Reliability and Trustworthiness Baseline*); or
      - [Article XV-A](core_04.020_rights_part_c.md#article-xv-a-security-intelligence-and-covert-power-limits) (*Security, Intelligence, and Covert-Power Limits*).
<a id="market-concentration-threshold-constitutional-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** Under the anti-nullification rule in [CJS-3.125 §11.1 — Market concentration threshold-setting discipline (adopter-tunable)](corpus_joint_structure/cjs_03.125_accountability_operations.md#cjs-3125-111-market-concentration-threshold-setting-discipline-adopter-tunable), it is non-compliant to:
    - set thresholds so high that they foreseeably never apply;
    - pair threshold rules with enforcement tools that are never used;
    - design a threshold regime that produces no real review or intervention; or
    - rely on an entity-count cosmetic, federated or shell packaging, or a cross-domain control swap that leaves real concentration unreviewed.
  - **Secondary failure:** It is non-compliant to:
    - **Evade through structure:** aggregate control through federated structures while still producing real concentration;
    - **Evade across domains:** substitute control in one domain for control in another to avoid a threshold;
    - **Ignore durable power:** design thresholds that disregard lasting domination, coercive dependency, or gatekeeper self-preferencing when those conditions materially degrade Contestability; or
    - **Delay heightened review:** design ceiling regimes that fail to trigger heightened review before consolidation predictably harms Contestability ([CJS-3.125 §11.3 Consolidation Ceiling-Setting Discipline (Adopter-Tunable)](corpus_joint_structure/cjs_03.125_accountability_operations.md#cjs-3125-113-consolidation-ceiling-setting-discipline-adopter-tunable)).

---

<a id="stakeholder-identification-participation-semi-independent"></a>

### Accountability: Dependent Clusters

<a id="defa1"></a>
<a id="collective-harm-boundary-and-harm-cluster"></a>

#### Def.A1 Collective Harm Boundary, Harm, and Harassment and Bullying

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Principles: [4 Safety (Harm Constraint)](core_02.000_values_principles.md#4-safety-harm-constraint), [13. Constitutional Collision Resolution Process](core_02.020_interaction_interpretation.md#13-constitutional-collision-resolution-process), and [Chapter Two §13.1.5 Rights-Collision Decision Test](core_02.020_interaction_interpretation.md#1315-rights-collision-decision-test) (2.020 §13.1.5).
- Read with:
  - [Materiality](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#materiality),
  - [Material Impact](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#material-impact),
  - [Risk](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#risk),
  - [Non-Imposition (Cooperative Interaction)](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#non-imposition-cooperative-interaction),
  - [Safety (Constitutional Constraint)](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#safety-constitutional-constraint),
  - [Redress and Remediation](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#redress-and-remediation), and
  - [Harm Minimization (Tradeoff Selection)](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#harm-minimization-tradeoff-selection), where materially implicated. Those definitions support this cluster but are not themselves cluster members unless another cluster or owner provision independently requires their joint invocation.

- Constitutional frame: **Accountability** leg; **Flourishing** aim (primary); scales with [material stake](core_00.000_preamble.md#material-stake) via [Materiality](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#materiality).
- Chapter Two basis: Chapter Two §15.1, §18.1, §19.1, §16 (see [Chapter Three compass](core_03.000_definitions_home.md#6-compass-and-definition-map) (3.000) map).
</details>

<br>

**Admission scope.** This cluster applies where a matter materially concerns:

- harmful effects or claimed harmlessness;
- collective or distributed harm;
- competing freedom or non-imposition claims;
- shared-resource, info-sphere, or systemic harm pathways;
- cruelty, suffering as an end in itself, or gratuitous or degrading infliction;
- harassment, bullying, or hostile-environment degradation; or
- the point at which protected action must be limited, contained, remediated, or redirected because it materially affects another sentient's protected interests or constitutionally protected shared conditions.

Outside that admission scope, component definitions may still operate as supporting Independent Definitions without importing the whole cluster.

Where admission scope is met, this cluster is the joint-invocation home for:

- [Harm](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#harm), as the general degradation concept;
- [Cruelty](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#cruelty), as the conduct-character construct for suffering inflicted as an end in itself or beyond necessity and proportionality;
- [Animal Cruelty](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#animal-cruelty), as the application of that construct to beings covered by the [Animal Life](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#animal-life) floor, including neglect by those responsible for their care;
- [Harassment and Bullying](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#harassment-and-bullying), as the patterned-conduct construct for unwanted conduct that materially degrades protected conditions in associational, institutional, dependency, and comparable cooperative settings; and
- [Collective Harm Boundary](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#collective-harm-boundary), as the defined limit on freedom of action: the point where one sentient's, collective's, or system's freedom of action must yield to verifiable harm affecting another sentient's protected interests or constitutionally protected shared conditions.

These definitions do different jobs, but they must be considered together when needed. When conduct harms others — whether directly, indirectly, over time, through accumulated effects, across systems, or at a collective level — the harm analysis must also ask whether the conduct crosses the [Collective Harm Boundary](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#collective-harm-boundary) and, where relevant, whether it amounts to [Cruelty](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#cruelty), harassment, or bullying.

**Cluster members.** This cluster comprises:

- [Harm](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#harm);
- [Psychological Harm](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#psychological-harm);
- [Irreversible Harm](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#irreversible-harm);
- [Cruelty](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#cruelty);
- [Animal Cruelty](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#animal-cruelty);
- [Harassment and Bullying](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#harassment-and-bullying);
- [Collective Harm Boundary](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#collective-harm-boundary).

**Anti-bypass.** Compartments: individual-harm, collective-harm, cruelty, harassment-and-bullying, non-imposition, materiality, freedom-limitation, and remediation. Separating them is non-compliant where it:

- recognizes a harm pathway while avoiding the [Collective Harm Boundary](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#collective-harm-boundary);
- invokes the [Collective Harm Boundary](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#collective-harm-boundary) while understating the harm;
- treats bare [Harm](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#harm) magnitude as a substitute for [Cruelty](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#cruelty) analysis where suffering-as-end or gratuitous infliction is materially implicated; or
- separates generic [Harm](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#harm) or the [Collective Harm Boundary](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#collective-harm-boundary) from [Harassment and Bullying](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#harassment-and-bullying) when patterned unwanted conduct is materially involved.

See **Anti-Segmentation Principle**.

The following limits also apply:

- Offense, preference, discomfort, or disagreement alone is not sufficient without a material harm pathway.
- A practice can still fall under this cluster even if it is described as private, local, expressive, commercial, technical, or collective. What matters is its real effect. The cluster applies when the practice causes material harm to protected interests or shared constitutional conditions — whether the harm is direct or indirect, appears later or builds over time, crosses systems, or affects the info-sphere, environment, wellbeing, or agency.

*Measurements (family routing):* Measured under the Accountability measurement family. Find the concrete measures on the member definitions below.

---

<a id="harm"></a>

##### Harm

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Flourishing measurement family (*Safety, harm, and risk as constitutional measurement*); Principles: [4 Safety (Harm Constraint)](core_02.000_values_principles.md#4-safety-harm-constraint).
- Read with: Wellbeing, Risk, Materiality, and Irreversible Harm.

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: harm is a material worsening of a sentient's life, agency, functioning, or psychological health. Offense, discomfort, or disagreement alone is not harm without a material causal pathway to such effects.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** Any effect that materially worsens survival, [Wellbeing](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#wellbeing), [Meaningful Agency](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#meaningful-agency), functional integrity, or [Psychological Harm](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#psychological-harm). This includes:
    - acute trauma;
    - long-term psychological destabilization; and
    - materially harmful neglect.
  - **Out of scope:** Offense, discomfort, or disagreement alone, without a material causal pathway to worsened survival, wellbeing, agency, functioning, or psychological health.
<a id="harm-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Flourishing measurement family](subcore_03.050_apex_flourishing_aim.md#measuring-flourishing-preamble-measurement-family) — *Are sentients sustained in life, safety, and access to essentials?*

    **Primary assessment:** Count:
    - direct;
    - indirect;
    - delayed;
    - cumulative; and
    - cross-system effects.

    Where material, also count psychologically mediated effects such as:
    - fear conditioning;
    - coercive [Dependency](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#dependency);
    - chronic intimidation;
    - sustained [Coercion and Manipulation](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#coercion-and-manipulation); and
    - deterioration caused by neglect.
  - **Secondary measure:** [Safety (Constitutional Constraint)](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#safety-constitutional-constraint), [Risk](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#risk), [Materiality](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#materiality), and [Irreversible Harm](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#irreversible-harm) — co-measures that scale harm assessment and constraint thresholds.

    **Secondary assessment:** When evaluating the primary trace, apply [Materiality](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#materiality), [Safety (Constitutional Constraint)](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#safety-constitutional-constraint), and [Risk](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#risk) to aggregation and distribution effects.
<a id="harm-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** dismissing harm because it is spread across many sentients or effects, even though [Materiality](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#materiality) would find the combined harm serious enough to count.
  - **Secondary failure:** dismissing harm to individuals without considering whether its collective, accumulated, or system-wide effects create a material harm pathway under [Safety (Constitutional Constraint)](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#safety-constitutional-constraint) or [Risk](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#risk).

---

<a id="psychological-harm"></a>

##### Psychological Harm

*In plain terms: psychological harm is a material injury to how a sentient thinks, feels, relates, or exercises agency. Ordinary discomfort is not enough.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** Material injury, destabilization, or worsening of a sentient's cognitive, emotional, or relational integrity caused by sentient or system behavior. Causes include:
    - institutional process;
    - coercion;
    - manipulation;
    - intimidation;
    - neglect; and
    - prolonged harmful conditions.

    Trauma and cumulative stress are included.
  - **Out of scope:** Ordinary discomfort that does not materially affect protected conditions.
