<a id="unified-incident"></a>

##### Unified Incident

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Canonical owner: [Chapter Fourteen §5](core_14.000_misconduct_designation.md#5-criteria-set-for-anti-constitutional-misconduct-designation--accountability-at-constitutional-scale) (20.000 §5).
- Read with: the
  - [Standing State, Contribution, and Violation cluster](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#defa3-standing-state-contribution-and-violation),
  - [Anti-Constitutional Misconduct Review](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#anti-constitutional-misconduct-review),
  - [Unified Incident Record](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#unified-incident-record),
  - [Materiality](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#materiality),
  - [System Boundaries](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#system-boundaries),
  - [System Boundary Integrity](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#system-boundary-integrity), and
  - [Accountability](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#accountability).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: a Unified Incident treats one materially connected episode, campaign, or pattern as a whole so it cannot be split into smaller pieces to hide its impact or avoid review.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:**
    - A whole episode, sustained campaign, or connected pattern assessed together for Chapter Twenty designation review.
    - The conduct must share material actors, objectives, continuity over time, causal links, or a unified design.
    - Treating it as one incident prevents artificial splitting that would understate satisfied criteria, verified impact, or accountability.
  - **Out of scope:**
    - unrelated events with no material connection;
    - caption-, docket-, or entity-boundary packaging alone.
<a id="unified-incident-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) and [Timeliness measurement family](subcore_03.065_apex_timeliness_leg.md#measuring-timeliness) — *Do reward structures, market power, and answerability keep duties real?* / *Are disputes, corrections, and repairs resolved while remedy still matters?*

    **Primary assessment:**
    - Draw the conduct boundary from real connections, not a caption, forum docket, entity boundary, transaction label, or implementation artifact.
    - When contest-pathway flooding and info-sphere flooding overlap, assess the shared facts in one unified incident record without counting the same conduct twice.
<a id="unified-incident-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** It is non-compliant to:
    - split one materially connected scheme into separate incidents to avoid Chapter Twenty criteria, gravity, review, publication, or remedy consequences; or
    - merge unrelated events solely to intensify classification without a material connection.

---

<a id="unified-record"></a>
<a id="unified-incident-record"></a>

##### Unified Incident Record

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Canonical owner: [Chapter Fourteen §5](core_14.000_misconduct_designation.md#5-criteria-set-for-anti-constitutional-misconduct-designation--accountability-at-constitutional-scale) (20.000 §5), [§6](core_14.000_misconduct_designation.md#6-violation-axis-slot-79-anti-constitutional-designation), and [Subcore 14-A — §4.3](subcore_14.010_misconduct_pattern_applications.md#43-info-sphere-zone-flooding).
- Read with: the
  - [Standing State, Contribution, and Violation cluster](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#defa3-standing-state-contribution-and-violation),
  - [Unified Incident](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#unified-incident),
  - [Anti-Constitutional Misconduct Review](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#anti-constitutional-misconduct-review),
  - [Auditability](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#auditability),
  - [Transparency](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#transparency),
  - [Verifiability](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#verifiability), and
  - [Standing Record](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#standing-record).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: a Unified Incident Record keeps the evidence and reasoning for one Unified Incident together while separately proving each criterion or pattern and protecting each subject's review rights.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:**
    - The combined evidence and reasoning used to assess a unified incident under Chapter Twenty's designation criteria and due-process safeguards after Subcore 10-D assigns the impact slot.
    - It keeps overlapping facts, actors, effects, criteria, and review status together while preserving separate proof for each criterion or pattern.
  - **Out of scope:**
    - ordinary separate case files for truly unrelated incidents; and
    - Chapter Sixteen character-descriptor attachments that do not assign or move the Subcore 10-D impact slot.
<a id="unified-record-a"></a>
<a id="unified-incident-record-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) and [Timeliness measurement family](subcore_03.065_apex_timeliness_leg.md#measuring-timeliness) — *Do reward structures, market power, and answerability keep duties real?* / *Are disputes, corrections, and repairs resolved while remedy still matters?*

    **Primary assessment:**
    - State the incident boundary, material facts, evidence source, implicated criteria, independently proven elements, review status, contest path, and overlap among named patterns.
    - Count a conduct element toward more than one Chapter Twenty criterion or named pattern only when the Unified Incident Record shows separate actions taken toward differing objectives — not when one undifferentiated course of conduct is given several labels.
<a id="unified-record-c"></a>
<a id="unified-incident-record-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** It is non-compliant to:
    - fragment records to hide pattern gravity;
    - merge records in a way that obscures distinct subjects or review rights;
    - count one undifferentiated course of conduct toward several Chapter Twenty criteria or named patterns without separate actions taken toward differing objectives;
    - use character descriptors to assign or move the Subcore 10-D impact slot, including:
      - intent;
      - duty;
      - negligence;
      - deception;
      - concealment;
      - obstruction;
      - coercion; or
      - process/response character.
    - issue a final Chapter Twenty designation without a reasoned, auditable unified incident record.

---

<a id="single-catastrophic-incident"></a>

##### Single Catastrophic Incident

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Canonical owner: [Chapter Fourteen §5](core_14.000_misconduct_designation.md#5-criteria-set-for-anti-constitutional-misconduct-designation--accountability-at-constitutional-scale) (20.000 §5) (*incident shapes for anti-constitutional-misconduct designation*).
- Read with: the
  - [Standing State, Contribution, and Violation cluster](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#defa3-standing-state-contribution-and-violation),
  - [Anti-Constitutional Misconduct Review](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#anti-constitutional-misconduct-review),
  - [Unified Incident](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#unified-incident),
  - [Violation Nature](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#violation-nature),
  - [Irreversible Harm](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#irreversible-harm), the Chapter Four Rights Floor, and
  - [Constitutional Constraint](subcore_03.080_band_integrative.md#constitutional-constraint).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: a Single Catastrophic Incident is one connected event whose verified impact causes or imminently threatens catastrophic constitutional harm.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:**
    - A unified incident that, taken as a whole, causes or imminently threatens catastrophic constitutional harm of the kind Subcore 10-D scores as **Grave Constitutional Impact** or **Catastrophic Constitutional Impact**. Chapter Twenty may attach the matching anti-constitutional-misconduct designation; it does not assign the numeric slot.
    - This includes loss or suspension of operative constitutional authority; material, population-scale loss of contestability or Rights-Floor continuity; or comparable harm relevant to survival or system continuity.
  - **Out of scope:**
    - serious but non-catastrophic violations; and
    - multiple unrelated serious events without one connected catastrophic episode.
<a id="single-catastrophic-incident-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) and [Timeliness measurement family](subcore_03.065_apex_timeliness_leg.md#measuring-timeliness) — *Do reward structures, market power, and answerability keep duties real?* / *Are disputes, corrections, and repairs resolved while remedy still matters?*

    **Primary assessment:**
    - Measure the verified impact as a whole under Subcore 10-D §8 (*Unified Proportional LEQU Scale — Contribution and Violation Axes*).
    - If the resulting slot is 7, 8, or 9, then apply Chapter Twenty's designation criteria and due-process safeguards.
    - Verified impact — not intent, another character descriptor, or the number of isolated acts — sets the numeric slot.
<a id="single-catastrophic-incident-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** It is non-compliant to:
    - use this label for serious violations that do not reach Subcore 10-D **slot 8 or 9** impact; or
    - avoid the label by splitting one catastrophic unified incident into smaller procedural units.

---

<a id="sustained-high-gravity-pattern"></a>

##### Sustained High-Gravity Pattern

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Canonical owner: [Chapter Fourteen §5](core_14.000_misconduct_designation.md#5-criteria-set-for-anti-constitutional-misconduct-designation--accountability-at-constitutional-scale) (20.000 §5) (*incident shapes for anti-constitutional-misconduct designation*).
- Read with: the
  - [Standing State, Contribution, and Violation cluster](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#defa3-standing-state-contribution-and-violation),
  - [Anti-Constitutional Misconduct Review](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#anti-constitutional-misconduct-review),
  - [Unified Incident](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#unified-incident),
  - [Unified Incident Record](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#unified-incident-record), Chapter Twenty criterion 3 (*Material rollback effect*),
  - [System Capture](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#system-capture),
  - [Review and Correction Duty](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#review-and-correction-duty), and
  - [Accountability](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#accountability).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: a Sustained High-Gravity Pattern is connected, continuing or repeated conduct whose combined verified impact reaches Subcore 10-D slot 7, 8, or 9, even without one catastrophic moment.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:**
    - A unified incident made up of continuing, repeated, or systematically reinforced conduct whose combined verified impact reaches Subcore 10-D slot 7, 8, or 9.
    - It may then undergo Chapter Twenty designation review.
    - Material rollback, blocked or chilled challenge rights, Rights-Floor degradation, correction delayed beyond practical remedy, an equivalent structural workaround, institutional scale, capture, and durability may describe the pattern and support designation analysis.
  - **Out of scope:**
    - ordinary repeated defects without combined constitutional harm reaching Subcore 10-D slot 7, 8, or 9; and
    - isolated one-off events without continuity or systematic reinforcement.
<a id="sustained-high-gravity-pattern-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Accountability measurement family](subcore_03.040_apex_accountability_leg.md#measuring-accountability) and [Timeliness measurement family](subcore_03.065_apex_timeliness_leg.md#measuring-timeliness) — *Do reward structures, market power, and answerability keep duties real?* / *Are disputes, corrections, and repairs resolved while remedy still matters?*

    **Primary assessment:**
    - Evaluate:
      - combined constitutional gravity;
      - continuation after a reasonable chance for good-faith resolution when resolution is feasible;
      - institutional scale;
      - capture;
      - durability; and
      - practical limits on remediation.
    - A pattern may reach Chapter Twenty gravity without a single catastrophic moment.
<a id="sustained-high-gravity-pattern-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** It is non-compliant to:
    - treat repeated high-gravity conduct as isolated minor events to avoid review; or
    - escalate ordinary repeated defects to Chapter Twenty gravity without proof of combined constitutional harm.

---

<a id="defa4"></a>
<a id="use-of-force-autonomous-coercion-and-mass-harm-cluster"></a>

#### Def.A4 Use of Force, Autonomous Coercion, Autonomous Lethal Systems, and Weapons of Mass Harm

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Constitutional frame: **Accountability** leg; **Continuity** aim (primary); scales with [material stake](core_00.000_preamble.md#material-stake) via [Materiality](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#materiality).
- Cross-leg note: integrative with **Continuity** where irreversible harm is implicated.
- Continuity disambiguation: Distinguish constitutional **Continuity** aim from operational force protocols.
- Chapter Two basis: Chapter Two §15.1, §18.1, §19.1, §16 (see [Chapter Three compass](core_03.000_definitions_home.md#6-compass-and-definition-map) (3.000) map).
- Read with: Apply
  - [Coercion and Manipulation](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#coercion-and-manipulation),
  - [Necessity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#necessity),
  - [Proportionality](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#proportionality),
  - [Reversibility](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#reversibility),
  - [Redress and Remediation](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#redress-and-remediation),
  - [Existential Risk](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#existential-risk),
  - [Safety (Constitutional Constraint)](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#safety-constitutional-constraint),
  - [Adversarial, Scaled, and Exploited Conditions](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#adversarial-scaled-and-exploited-conditions), and
  - [Sentience Non-Exclusion](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#sentience-non-exclusion) where materially implicated.

</details>

<br>


**Admission scope.** This cluster applies where a matter materially concerns:

- overt force deployment or targeting discipline;
- weapons whose harm scale implicates environmental or existential-risk scrutiny; or
- autonomous lethal or coercive systems (including coercion pathways that intersect those tools).

Outside that admission scope, individual definitions may operate as supporting Independent Definitions without importing the whole cluster.

Where admission scope is met, this cluster is the joint-invocation home for overt force, weapons of mass harm, autonomous lethal and coercion systems, and combatant / non-combatant discipline under **Article XV-B** (*Use of Force, Armed Conflict, and Military-Power Limits*) and **Article XV-C** (*Autonomous Lethal Systems and Autonomous Coercion Tools*), including adjacent existential-risk, reversibility, and redress interfaces where those are materially interdependent.

**Cluster members.** This cluster comprises:

- [Use of Force](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#use-of-force);
- [Autonomous Lethal System](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#autonomous-lethal-system);
- [Weapons of Mass Harm](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#weapons-of-mass-harm);
- [Combatant / Non-Combatant Distinction](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#combatant--non-combatant-distinction);
- [Irreversible Deprivation Measure](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#irreversible-deprivation-measure), for non-conflation discipline with **Article XXI-B** (*Restriction Floors*);
- [Autonomous Coercion Tool](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#autonomous-coercion-tool).

**Anti-bypass.** Compartments: authorization, targeting, weapons-class, autonomy, coercion, and review. Separating them is non-compliant where it satisfies one component while defeating materially interdependent force, discrimination, autonomy-control, or non-conflation duties. See [§15.1.1 Anti-Segmentation Principle](core_02.030_constitutional_interpretation.md#1511-anti-segmentation-principle).

See **Anti-Segmentation Principle**.

*Measurements (family routing):* Measured under the Accountability measurement family. Find the concrete measures on the member definitions below.

<a id="use-of-force-constitutional"></a>

##### Use of Force

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Principles: [13.1 Core Tradeoff Principles](core_02.020_interaction_interpretation.md#131-core-tradeoff-principles) (**Necessity**, **Proportionality**, heightened scrutiny); [13. Constitutional Collision Resolution Process](core_02.020_interaction_interpretation.md#13-constitutional-collision-resolution-process) (emergency / escalation read-with **Article XV-B** (*Use of Force, Armed Conflict, and Military-Power Limits*)).
- Owner floor: [Article XV-B](core_04.020_rights_part_c.md#article-xv-b-use-of-force-armed-conflict-and-military-power-limits) (*Use of Force, Armed Conflict, and Military-Power Limits*). Overt-power counterpart to the covert-power limits of [Article XV-A](core_04.020_rights_part_c.md#article-xv-a-security-intelligence-and-covert-power-limits) (*Security, Intelligence, and Covert-Power Limits*). Non-conflation: this entry does not authorize, extend, or predicate involuntary deprivation of life under **Article XXI-B** (*Categorical prohibition of irreversible deprivation of life as a justice measure*, as revised); read with [Irreversible Deprivation Measure](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#irreversible-deprivation-measure).
- Cluster component: [Def.A4 *Use of Force, Autonomous Coercion, Autonomous Lethal Systems, and Weapons of Mass Harm*](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#defa4-use-of-force-autonomous-coercion-autonomous-lethal-systems-and-weapons-of-mass-harm).
- Read with:
  - [Weapons of Mass Harm](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#weapons-of-mass-harm),
  - [Combatant / Non-Combatant Distinction](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#combatant--non-combatant-distinction),
  - [Autonomous Lethal System](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#autonomous-lethal-system),
  - [Irreversible Deprivation Measure](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#irreversible-deprivation-measure),
  - [Necessity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#necessity),
  - [Proportionality](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#proportionality),
  - [Reversibility](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#reversibility),
  - [Redress and Remediation](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#redress-and-remediation), and
  - [Existential Risk](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#existential-risk).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: Use of Force means overt physical, coercive, or kinetic force by an institution or comparable actor. It is exceptional and must remain lawful, necessary, proportionate, narrow, time-bounded, and reviewable.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** Overt application of physical, coercive, or kinetic force by institutions, stewards, operators, or comparable actors, including:
    - security, policing, conflict, and military-power deployment under the registers that [Article XV-B](core_04.020_rights_part_c.md#article-xv-b-use-of-force-armed-conflict-and-military-power-limits) (*Use of Force, Armed Conflict, and Military-Power Limits*) covers; and
    - application to both force-users and force-affected sentients.

    Use of Force is constitutionally exceptional.
  - **Depends on:** [Sentience Non-Exclusion](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#sentience-non-exclusion), [Necessity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#necessity), and [Proportionality](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#proportionality) — validity bounds. Force is valid only where:
    - authority is lawful and published;
    - the objective is constitutionally legitimate and materially serious;
    - less harmful means are not reasonably sufficient; and
    - the use remains necessity-compatible, proportionality-compatible, narrowly tailored, time-bounded, and independently reviewable.
  - **Out of scope:**
    - ordinary non-coercive conduct; and
    - force used outside the registers **Article XV-B** (*Use of Force, Armed Conflict, and Military-Power Limits*) covers.
<a id="use-of-force-constitutional-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Flourishing measurement family](subcore_03.050_apex_flourishing_aim.md#measuring-flourishing-preamble-measurement-family) and [Continuity measurement family](subcore_03.045_apex_continuity_aim.md#measuring-continuity-preamble-measurement-family) — *Are sentients sustained in life, safety, and access to essentials?* / *Can sentients and systems endure — ecologically, dependably, and across failure?*

    **Primary assessment:** Check:

    - that authorization is lawful and published;
    - that the objective is legitimate and materially serious;
    - that less-harmful means are not reasonably sufficient;
    - [Necessity](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#necessity), [Proportionality](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#proportionality), and narrow tailoring throughout the use;
    - targeting under [Combatant / Non-Combatant Distinction](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#combatant--non-combatant-distinction);
    - [Adversarial, Scaled, and Exploited Conditions](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#adversarial-scaled-and-exploited-conditions) when scaled or adversarial deployment is foreseeable;
    - [Existential Risk](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#existential-risk) when [Weapons of Mass Harm](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#weapons-of-mass-harm) are materially implicated; and
    - outcome discipline under [Reversibility](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#reversibility) and [Redress and Remediation](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#redress-and-remediation).

    An emergency label fails this test if indefinite extension or routine reauthorization makes force normal in practice.
<a id="use-of-force-constitutional-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** Using force without any of the following under [Article XV-B](core_04.020_rights_part_c.md#article-xv-b-use-of-force-armed-conflict-and-military-power-limits) (*Use of Force, Armed Conflict, and Military-Power Limits*):
    - lawful published authority;
    - a legitimate and materially serious objective;
    - proof that less-harmful means are insufficient; or
    - ongoing Necessity and Proportionality.
  - **Secondary failure:**
    - denial-of-quarter;
    - collective retaliation;
    - protected-characteristic-based targeting; or
    - substrate-class compulsion into combatant status.
  - **Tertiary failure:**
    - emergency-dressed normalization through indefinite extension or routinized reauthorization without substantive review (**Chapter Twenty-Three §5.1** (*Emergency Measures and Continuation Burden*) and this entry); or
    - reading this entry as a predicate for, or alignment with, irreversible involuntary deprivation outside combat — such measures are categorically prohibited under **Article XXI-B** (*Restriction Floors*) as revised and are governed with [Irreversible Deprivation Measure](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#irreversible-deprivation-measure).

---

<a id="autonomous-lethal-system-constitutional"></a>

##### Autonomous Lethal System

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Principles: [2.1. Non-Negotiable Constraints: Safety and Truth](core_02.000_values_principles.md#21-non-negotiable-principle-constraints-safety-and-truth); [13.1.3 Proportionality](core_02.020_interaction_interpretation.md#1313-proportionality) (irreversible harm and existential-risk index).
- Owner floor: [Article XV-C](core_04.020_rights_part_c.md#article-xv-c-autonomous-lethal-systems-and-autonomous-coercion-tools) (*Autonomous Lethal Systems and Autonomous Coercion Tools*). Existential-risk interaction: [Article I-D](core_04.000_rights_part_a.md#article-i-d-existential-risk-and-ecological-recovery-capacity) (*Existential Risk and Ecological Recovery Capacity*). Systems-layer implementation: [Article XIV-A](core_04.020_rights_part_c.md#article-xiv-a-reliability-and-trustworthiness-baseline) (*Reliability and Trustworthiness Baseline*).
- Cluster component: [Def.A4 *Use of Force, Autonomous Coercion, Autonomous Lethal Systems, and Weapons of Mass Harm*](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#defa4-use-of-force-autonomous-coercion-autonomous-lethal-systems-and-weapons-of-mass-harm).
- Read with:
  - [Autonomous Coercion Tool](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#autonomous-coercion-tool),
  - [Use of Force](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#use-of-force),
  - [Weapons of Mass Harm](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#weapons-of-mass-harm),
  - [Combatant / Non-Combatant Distinction](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#combatant--non-combatant-distinction),
  - [Existential Risk](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#existential-risk),
  - [Adversarial, Scaled, and Exploited Conditions](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#adversarial-scaled-and-exploited-conditions),
  - [Reversibility](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#reversibility),
  - [Irreversible Deprivation Measure](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#irreversible-deprivation-measure),
  - [Redress and Remediation](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#redress-and-remediation), and
  - [Subcore 3-B §4.1.1 Formal Label and Representation Gaming](subcore_03.020_definition_integrity.md#411-formal-label-and-representation-gaming) (S3-B §4.1.1).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: an Autonomous Lethal System selects, engages, or materially directs lethal-force targets without real and timely human judgment shaping every targeting or engagement decision.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope — substrates:** A system that, under actual operating conditions, selects, engages, or materially directs lethal force without a human's real and timely judgment shaping each targeting or engagement decision, including:
    - autonomous platforms;
    - swarms;
    - software-mediated targeting pipelines; and
    - hybrid arrangements.
  - **Depends on:** [Use of Force](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#use-of-force) and [Combatant / Non-Combatant Distinction](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#combatant--non-combatant-distinction) — constitutive bounds under [Article XV-C](core_04.020_rights_part_c.md#article-xv-c-autonomous-lethal-systems-and-autonomous-coercion-tools) (*Autonomous Lethal Systems and Autonomous Coercion Tools*). Deployment and each targeting decision must satisfy:
    - Use of Force validity; and
    - Combatant / Non-Combatant Distinction discipline.
  - **Out of scope:**
    - systems where a human's real, timely judgment materially shapes each targeting or engagement decision; and
    - use outside targeting or lethal engagement.
<a id="autonomous-lethal-system-constitutional-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Flourishing measurement family](subcore_03.050_apex_flourishing_aim.md#measuring-flourishing-preamble-measurement-family) and [Continuity measurement family](subcore_03.045_apex_continuity_aim.md#measuring-continuity-preamble-measurement-family) — *Are sentients sustained in life, safety, and access to essentials?* / *Can sentients and systems endure — ecologically, dependably, and across failure?*

    **Primary assessment:** Judge what the system actually does, not what it is called. At minimum, check:

    - that a human's substantive judgment materially shapes each targeting or engagement decision at operational speed, rather than merely approving it afterward;
    - classification accuracy, robustness under [Adversarial, Scaled, and Exploited Conditions](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#adversarial-scaled-and-exploited-conditions), and failure-mode behavior independently satisfying [Combatant / Non-Combatant Distinction](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#combatant--non-combatant-distinction);
    - whether capability, scale, or deployment conditions materially implicate [Existential Risk](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#existential-risk), triggering [Article I-D](core_04.000_rights_part_a.md#article-i-d-existential-risk-and-ecological-recovery-capacity) (*Existential Risk and Ecological Recovery Capacity*) scrutiny;
    - whether recovery, override, and intervention pathways under [Self-Healing](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#self-healing) and [Article XIV-F](core_04.020_rights_part_c.md#article-xiv-f-resilience-and-self-healing-baseline) (*Resilience and Self-Healing Baseline*) are substantive, not nominal; and
    - outcome discipline under [Reversibility](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#reversibility) and [Redress and Remediation](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#redress-and-remediation) for each application.
<a id="autonomous-lethal-system-constitutional-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** Deploying or using an Autonomous Lethal System under [Article XV-C](core_04.020_rights_part_c.md#article-xv-c-autonomous-lethal-systems-and-autonomous-coercion-tools) (*Autonomous Lethal Systems and Autonomous Coercion Tools*) when:
    - a human's substantive judgment does not materially shape each targeting or engagement decision; or
    - operator-intent framing is used to mask the absence of that judgment.
  - **Secondary failure:** Deploying or using a system without independently proving that it satisfies [Combatant / Non-Combatant Distinction](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#combatant--non-combatant-distinction) under:
    - adversarial conditions; and
    - scaled conditions.
  - **Tertiary failure:** Treating such a system as ordinary capability-expansion rather than an [Article I-D](core_04.000_rights_part_a.md#article-i-d-existential-risk-and-ecological-recovery-capacity) (*Existential Risk and Ecological Recovery Capacity*) object when existential-risk thresholds are materially implicated.

---

<a id="weapons-of-mass-harm-constitutional"></a>

##### Weapons of Mass Harm

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Principles: [2.1. Non-Negotiable Constraints: Safety and Truth](core_02.000_values_principles.md#21-non-negotiable-principle-constraints-safety-and-truth); [13.1.3 Proportionality](core_02.020_interaction_interpretation.md#1313-proportionality) (**Existential Risk**, **Irreversible Harm**, cross-system harm scale).
- Owner floor: [Article XV-B](core_04.020_rights_part_c.md#article-xv-b-use-of-force-armed-conflict-and-military-power-limits) (*Use of Force, Armed Conflict, and Military-Power Limits*). Existential-risk interaction: [Article I-D](core_04.000_rights_part_a.md#article-i-d-existential-risk-and-ecological-recovery-capacity) (*Existential Risk and Ecological Recovery Capacity*). Environmental-preconditions interaction: [Article I-A](core_04.000_rights_part_a.md#article-i-a-environmental-preconditions-and-ecological-integrity) (*Environmental Preconditions and Ecological Integrity*).
- Cluster component: [Def.A4 *Use of Force, Autonomous Coercion, Autonomous Lethal Systems, and Weapons of Mass Harm*](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#defa4-use-of-force-autonomous-coercion-autonomous-lethal-systems-and-weapons-of-mass-harm).
- Read with:
  - [Use of Force](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#use-of-force),
  - [Existential Risk](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#existential-risk),
  - [Reversibility](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#reversibility),
  - [Cascading Failure](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#cascading-failure),
  - [Ecological Integrity](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#ecological-integrity),
  - [Info-Sphere](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#info-sphere),
  - [Irreversible Deprivation Measure](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#irreversible-deprivation-measure), and
  - [Redress and Remediation](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#redress-and-remediation).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: Weapons of Mass Harm are defined by the foreseeable scale of casualty, ecological, informational, or infrastructure harm they can cause—not by a weapon taxonomy or label.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** Weapons, weapon systems, or deliberately weaponized capabilities whose foreseeable use causes casualty, ecological, informational, or infrastructural harm at a scale that materially implicates:
    - environmental-preconditions discipline under **Article I-A** (*Environmental Preconditions and Ecological Integrity*);
    - existential-risk scrutiny under **Article I-D** (*Existential Risk and Ecological Recovery Capacity*); or
    - both.

    Foreseeable harm scale and the burden on ecological recovery capacity control, not weapon-class taxonomy alone. Covered harm pathways include, regardless of substrate:
    - biological-effect;
    - synthetic-effect;
    - informational;
    - infrastructural; and
    - hybrid.
  - **Depends on:** [Use of Force](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#use-of-force), [Existential Risk](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#existential-risk), and [Ecological Integrity](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#ecological-integrity) — constitutive bounds. Possession, transfer, deployment, or use must satisfy:
    - Use of Force discipline; and
    - the harm-scale thresholds these entries supply.
  - **Out of scope:**
    - ordinary force tools whose foreseeable harm scale does not implicate **Article I-A** (*Environmental Preconditions and Ecological Integrity*) or **Article I-D** (*Existential Risk and Ecological Recovery Capacity*) thresholds; or
    - weapon-class taxonomy alone.
<a id="weapons-of-mass-harm-constitutional-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Flourishing measurement family](subcore_03.050_apex_flourishing_aim.md#measuring-flourishing-preamble-measurement-family) and [Continuity measurement family](subcore_03.045_apex_continuity_aim.md#measuring-continuity-preamble-measurement-family) — *Are sentients sustained in life, safety, and access to essentials?* / *Can sentients and systems endure — ecologically, dependably, and across failure?*

    **Primary assessment:** Assess foreseeable harm under [Adversarial, Scaled, and Exploited Conditions](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#adversarial-scaled-and-exploited-conditions), covering:

    - casualty harm;
    - ecological harm;
    - informational harm; and
    - infrastructure harm.

    Also apply:

    - [Cascading Failure](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#cascading-failure) when system-wide spread is plausible;
    - [Existential Risk](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#existential-risk) when **Article I-D** (*Existential Risk and Ecological Recovery Capacity*) thresholds are materially implicated; and
    - [Reversibility](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#reversibility) to the recovery path.

    Do not treat a weapon that meets these harm-scale thresholds as an ordinary force-escalation tool.
<a id="weapons-of-mass-harm-constitutional-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** Possessing, transferring, deploying, or using Weapons of Mass Harm without meeting each of the following where materially implicated:
    - [Article XV-B](core_04.020_rights_part_c.md#article-xv-b-use-of-force-armed-conflict-and-military-power-limits) (*Use of Force, Armed Conflict, and Military-Power Limits*) force rules;
    - [Article I-A](core_04.000_rights_part_a.md#article-i-a-environmental-preconditions-and-ecological-integrity) (*Environmental Preconditions and Ecological Integrity*) environmental-preconditions rules; and
    - [Article I-D](core_04.000_rights_part_a.md#article-i-d-existential-risk-and-ecological-recovery-capacity) (*Existential Risk and Ecological Recovery Capacity*) scrutiny.
  - **Secondary failure:** Claiming that **Article I-D** (*Existential Risk and Ecological Recovery Capacity*) does not apply based only on weapon taxonomy when the foreseeable harm scale reaches its thresholds.

---

<a id="combatant-non-combatant-distinction-constitutional"></a>

##### Combatant / Non-Combatant Distinction

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Principles: [13.1.3 Proportionality](core_02.020_interaction_interpretation.md#1313-proportionality); [Chapter Two §13.1.5 Rights-Collision Decision Test](core_02.020_interaction_interpretation.md#1315-rights-collision-decision-test) (2.020 §13.1.5) (combatant-status doubt and non-combatant protection).
- Owner floor: [Article XV-B](core_04.020_rights_part_c.md#article-xv-b-use-of-force-armed-conflict-and-military-power-limits) (*Use of Force, Armed Conflict, and Military-Power Limits*); interacts with [Article XV-C](core_04.020_rights_part_c.md#article-xv-c-autonomous-lethal-systems-and-autonomous-coercion-tools) (*Autonomous Lethal Systems and Autonomous Coercion Tools*) on autonomous-targeting compliance.
- Cluster component: [Def.A4 *Use of Force, Autonomous Coercion, Autonomous Lethal Systems, and Weapons of Mass Harm*](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#defa4-use-of-force-autonomous-coercion-autonomous-lethal-systems-and-weapons-of-mass-harm).
- Read with:
  - [Use of Force](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#use-of-force),
  - [Weapons of Mass Harm](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#weapons-of-mass-harm),
  - [Autonomous Lethal System](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#autonomous-lethal-system),
  - [Protected Characteristics](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#protected-characteristics),
  - [Protected Characteristic Proxying and Disparate Impact](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#protected-characteristic-proxying-and-disparate-impact),
  - [Substantive Fairness](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#substantive-fairness), and
  - [Proportionality](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#proportionality).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: combatant status depends on what an individual is doing at the time of the force decision. It cannot be assigned from demographic groups, protected characteristics, proxies, or convenient labels.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** Individual-level distinction, at the time of each applicable use-of-force decision, between:
    - sentients who are directly taking part in hostilities, armed action, or active coercive-force conduct (combatants); and
    - those who are not (non-combatants).

    Formal combatant-class assignment is relevant evidence, but:
    - it cannot establish combatant status where it conflicts with substantive conduct; and
    - substantive non-combatant conduct is not defeated by adverse formal classification.

    Does not authorize population-level reclassification by:
    - demographic features;
    - protected-characteristic features; or
    - proxy features.
  - **Depends on:** [Sentience Non-Exclusion](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#sentience-non-exclusion) and [Use of Force](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#use-of-force) — constitutive bounds. The distinction:
    - applies to all sentients without substrate-class narrowing; and
    - operates only within applicable Use of Force decisions.
  - **Out of scope:**
    - demographic-group assignment;
    - protected-characteristic assignment; or
    - formal class assignment

    — each without individual conduct at the time of the force decision.
<a id="combatant-non-combatant-distinction-constitutional-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Flourishing measurement family](subcore_03.050_apex_flourishing_aim.md#measuring-flourishing-preamble-measurement-family) and [Continuity measurement family](subcore_03.045_apex_continuity_aim.md#measuring-continuity-preamble-measurement-family) — *Are sentients sustained in life, safety, and access to essentials?* / *Can sentients and systems endure — ecologically, dependably, and across failure?*

    **Primary assessment:** Check:

    - the individual's actual conduct at the relevant time, not only a formal class designation;
    - whether a convenient reclassification sweeps protected populations into combatant status;
    - whether the classification uses [Protected Characteristics](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#protected-characteristics) or material proxies under [Protected Characteristic Proxying and Disparate Impact](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#protected-characteristic-proxying-and-disparate-impact); and
    - whether doubt resolves toward non-combatant status, consistent with [Proportionality](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#proportionality) and [Substantive Fairness](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#substantive-fairness).

    For autonomous systems, evaluate under [Article XV-C](core_04.020_rights_part_c.md#article-xv-c-autonomous-lethal-systems-and-autonomous-coercion-tools) (*Autonomous Lethal Systems and Autonomous Coercion Tools*):
    - classification accuracy; and
    - failure modes under [Adversarial, Scaled, and Exploited Conditions](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#adversarial-scaled-and-exploited-conditions).
<a id="combatant-non-combatant-distinction-constitutional-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:**
    - denial-of-quarter;
    - collective retaliation;
    - targeting based on protected characteristics or material proxies;
    - convenient reclassifications that sweep protected populations into combatant status; or
    - demographic-group, protected-characteristic, or formal class assignment used as combatant status without individual conduct at the time of the force decision.
  - **Secondary failure:** Autonomous-system classification whose accuracy or failure behavior does not independently satisfy this distinction under [Article XV-C](core_04.020_rights_part_c.md#article-xv-c-autonomous-lethal-systems-and-autonomous-coercion-tools) (*Autonomous Lethal Systems and Autonomous Coercion Tools*) when tested under:
    - adversarial conditions; and
    - scaled conditions.

---

<a id="irreversible-deprivation-measure-constitutional"></a>

##### Irreversible Deprivation Measure

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Principles: [3. Foundational Objective: Wellbeing](core_02.000_values_principles.md#3-foundational-objective-wellbeing-flourishing-aim); [14. Prohibition on Absolute Override](core_02.020_interaction_interpretation.md#14-prohibition-on-absolute-override) (non-trivial restriction and dignity floor read-with **Article XXI-B** (*Restriction Floors*)).
- Owner floor: [Article XXI-B](core_04.030_rights_part_d.md#article-xxi-b-restriction-floors) (*Categorical prohibition of irreversible deprivation of life as a justice measure*). Interaction pointers: the least-restrictive and time-bounded floor in the same Article (durable-containment discipline); [Article XXVIII](core_04.040_rights_part_e.md#article-xxviii-constitutional-evolution-and-non-entrenchment) (*Constitutional Evolution and Non-Entrenchment*) non-entrenchment; [Article XXIX](core_04.040_rights_part_e.md#article-xxix-adoption-transition-governance-continuity-and-re-baselining) (*Adoption Transition Governance, Continuity, and Re-Baselining*) Adoption Transition governance and "no durable exception authority" rule.

  - [Reversibility](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#reversibility),
  - [Redress and Remediation](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#redress-and-remediation),
  - [Dignity and Equal Moral Standing](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#dignity-and-equal-moral-standing),
  - [Voluntary Discontinuation](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#voluntary-discontinuation),
  - [Use of Force](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#use-of-force),
  - [Weapons of Mass Harm](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#weapons-of-mass-harm),
  - [Autonomous Lethal System](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#autonomous-lethal-system),
  - [Autonomous Coercion Tool](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#autonomous-coercion-tool),
  - [Sentience Non-Exclusion](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#sentience-non-exclusion), and
  - [Irreversible Harm](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#irreversible-harm).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: an Irreversible Deprivation Measure is a justice measure that irreversibly deprives a sentient, especially of life. It is categorically prohibited and remains distinct from reversible containment, Voluntary Discontinuation, and force regulation.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope:** Justice measures whose imposition produces irreversible deprivation of a sentient, including:
    - paradigmatically, deprivation of life by a state, operator, or comparable actor as a justice measure; and
    - analytically, any measure whose effect is foreclosed to [Reversibility](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#reversibility) and [Redress and Remediation](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#redress-and-remediation).

    Applies under [Sentience Non-Exclusion](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#sentience-non-exclusion).
  - **Depends on:** Constitutive bounds for what counts as irreversible deprivation and who is protected:
    - [Sentience Non-Exclusion](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#sentience-non-exclusion);
    - [Reversibility](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#reversibility); and
    - [Redress and Remediation](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#redress-and-remediation).
  - **Out of scope:**
    - durable but reversible containment under [Article XXI-B](core_04.030_rights_part_d.md#article-xxi-b-restriction-floors) (*Restriction Floors*), subject to its duration-limit, review-cadence, and restoration discipline;
    - [Voluntary Discontinuation](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#voluntary-discontinuation) (the sentient's own freely-formed decision under [Article VII-D](core_04.010_rights_part_b.md#article-vii-d-voluntary-discontinuation-of-ones-own-existence) (*Voluntary Discontinuation of One's Own Existence*)); and
    - overt [Use of Force](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#use-of-force), [Weapons of Mass Harm](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#weapons-of-mass-harm), and [Autonomous Lethal System](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#autonomous-lethal-system) / [Autonomous Coercion Tool](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#autonomous-coercion-tool) operation governed by Articles XV-B / XV-C under their own owner-floor discipline even where effects are irreversible, without read-across authorizing a deprivation measure.
<a id="irreversible-deprivation-measure-measurements"></a>
<a id="irreversible-deprivation-measure-constitutional-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Flourishing measurement family](subcore_03.050_apex_flourishing_aim.md#measuring-flourishing-preamble-measurement-family) and [Continuity measurement family](subcore_03.045_apex_continuity_aim.md#measuring-continuity-preamble-measurement-family) — *Are sentients sustained in life, safety, and access to essentials?* / *Can sentients and systems endure — ecologically, dependably, and across failure?*

    **Primary assessment:** Test what the measure does in substance: does its effect foreclose [Reversibility](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#reversibility) and [Redress and Remediation](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#redress-and-remediation), regardless of its procedural form? Detect:

    - re-introduction framings that route the prohibited measure through:
      - Subcore 10-D impact slots or Chapter Twenty designation ("final slot 8 / slot 9" predicates);
      - **Chapter Twenty-Three §5.1** (*Emergency Measures and Continuation Burden*) emergency measures;
      - **Article XXIX** (*Adoption Transition Governance, Continuity, and Re-Baselining*) Adoption Transition or re-baselining governance;
      - adopter-document custody; or
      - comparable mechanisms;
    - "rehabilitation infeasibility" or "less-restrictive measures cannot achieve safety" framings used to carry the prohibited measure past the categorical prohibition in **Article XXI-B** (*Restriction Floors*);
    - use of a nominally reversible durable-containment regime under **Article XXI-B** (*Restriction Floors*) as a predicate for the prohibited measure; and
    - re-labelling of an involuntarily-imposed outcome as "voluntary" to route around this entry into **Article VII-D** (*Voluntary Discontinuation of One's Own Existence*):
      - a sentient's own freely-formed decision remains governed by **Article VII-D** (*Voluntary Discontinuation of One's Own Existence*) and [Voluntary Discontinuation](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#voluntary-discontinuation); and
      - any conversion of that decision into a non-voluntary outcome by state, operator, or comparable actor returns the question to this entry.

    Distinguish this entry from:
    - [Use of Force](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#use-of-force);
    - [Weapons of Mass Harm](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#weapons-of-mass-harm);
    - [Autonomous Lethal System](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#autonomous-lethal-system); and
    - [Autonomous Coercion Tool](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#autonomous-coercion-tool).

    Those entries govern force and weapons discipline under their own owner floors without authorizing deprivation measures. Apply substrate-agnostically:
    - [Sentience Non-Exclusion](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#sentience-non-exclusion);
    - [Dignity and Equal Moral Standing](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#dignity-and-equal-moral-standing); and
    - [Irreversible Harm](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#irreversible-harm).

    Substrate-class narrowing (e.g., "synthetic-instance termination is not deprivation of life within this entry") fails this evaluation.
<a id="irreversible-deprivation-measure-constitutional-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** A state, operator, or comparable actor imposes irreversible deprivation of life as a justice measure — non-compliant without exception under revised **Article XXI-B** (*Restriction Floors*).
  - **Secondary failure:**
    - routing the prohibited measure through Subcore 10-D impact assignment, Chapter Twenty designation, emergency framing, Adoption Transition or re-baselining governance, or adopter-document custody to reintroduce it;
    - using lasting but reversible containment under **Article XXI-B** (*Restriction Floors*) as a stepping-stone or justification for the prohibited measure; or
    - re-labelling an involuntarily-imposed outcome as "voluntary discontinuation" to route into **Article VII-D** (*Voluntary Discontinuation of One's Own Existence*).
  - **Tertiary failure:** Substrate-class narrowings on taxonomy grounds alone ([Sentience Non-Exclusion](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#sentience-non-exclusion)).

---

<a id="autonomous-coercion-tool-constitutional"></a>

##### Autonomous Coercion Tool

<details>
<summary><strong><span style="color: #2563eb;">Trace</span></strong></summary>

- Downstream: Principles: [2.1. Non-Negotiable Constraints: Safety and Truth](core_02.000_values_principles.md#21-non-negotiable-principle-constraints-safety-and-truth); [Subcore 8-B §4 Whole-System Certification Evaluation](subcore_08.020_system_alignment_certification_evaluation.md#4-whole-system-certification-evaluation) (S8-B §4) through [Subcore 8-B §6 Rights-Floor and Domain Evaluations](subcore_08.020_system_alignment_certification_evaluation.md#6-rights-floor-and-domain-evaluations) (scaled / adversarial deployment scrutiny).
- Owner floor: [Article XV-C](core_04.020_rights_part_c.md#article-xv-c-autonomous-lethal-systems-and-autonomous-coercion-tools) (*Autonomous Lethal Systems and Autonomous Coercion Tools*). Systems-layer implementation: [Article XIV-A](core_04.020_rights_part_c.md#article-xiv-a-reliability-and-trustworthiness-baseline) (*Reliability and Trustworthiness Baseline*) and [CS-8.305](corpus_systems/cs_08.305_system_classification_machinery.md#cs-8305-system-classification-machinery) (*System classification machinery*) and [Subcore 8-A — System Classification](subcore_08.010_system_classification.md#subcore-8-a-system-classification) classification.
- Cluster component: [Def.A4 *Use of Force, Autonomous Coercion, Autonomous Lethal Systems, and Weapons of Mass Harm*](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#defa4-use-of-force-autonomous-coercion-autonomous-lethal-systems-and-weapons-of-mass-harm).
- Read with:
  - [Autonomous Lethal System](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#autonomous-lethal-system),
  - [Coercion and Manipulation](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#coercion-and-manipulation),
  - [Trustworthiness](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#trustworthiness),
  - [Adversarial, Scaled, and Exploited Conditions](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#adversarial-scaled-and-exploited-conditions),
  - [Reversibility](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#reversibility),
  - [Irreversible Deprivation Measure](subcore_03.070_band_accountability.md#irreversible-deprivation-measure),
  - [Meaningful Agency](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#meaningful-agency),
  - [Protected Characteristics](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#protected-characteristics), and
  - [Subcore 3-B §4.1.1 Formal Label and Representation Gaming](subcore_03.020_definition_integrity.md#411-formal-label-and-representation-gaming) (S3-B §4.1.1).

</details>

<br>

*In plain terms: an Autonomous Coercion Tool restricts, pressures, or manipulates sentients — including through untrustworthy or arbitrary behavior and demands — without real and timely human judgment shaping every coercive application.*

- **What it is**
  - **In scope — coercive effects:** A system that constrains sentients' freedom or decisions through autonomous or adaptive behavior, without a human's real and timely judgment shaping each coercive application, including:
    - shaping behavior at scale;
    - targeted restriction of movement or access;
    - targeted suppression or chilling of expression;
    - targeted manipulation as defined in [Coercion and Manipulation](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#coercion-and-manipulation);
    - untrustworthy or arbitrary behavior and demands that constrain freedom or decisions;
    - targeted harm calibrated by protected characteristics or their material proxies; and
    - infliction of psychological trauma, acute stress, or chronic psychological destabilization.

    Substrate-agnostic, covering both non-lethal and lethal coercive effects.
  - **Depends on:** [Sentience Non-Exclusion](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#sentience-non-exclusion), [Meaningful Agency](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#meaningful-agency), and [Trustworthiness](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#trustworthiness) — constitutive bounds under [Article XV-C](core_04.020_rights_part_c.md#article-xv-c-autonomous-lethal-systems-and-autonomous-coercion-tools) (*Autonomous Lethal Systems and Autonomous Coercion Tools*). Coercive scope must not defeat:
    - substrate-agnostic protection;
    - agency floors; or
    - justified-reliance and non-arbitrary conduct under [Trustworthiness](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#trustworthiness).
  - **Out of scope:**
    - systems that automate permitted tasks without coercing sentients; or
    - systems where a human's real, timely judgment shapes each coercive application.
<a id="autonomous-coercion-tool-constitutional-a"></a>
- **How to measure and assess**
  - **Primary measure:** [Flourishing measurement family](subcore_03.050_apex_flourishing_aim.md#measuring-flourishing-preamble-measurement-family) and [Continuity measurement family](subcore_03.045_apex_continuity_aim.md#measuring-continuity-preamble-measurement-family) — *Are sentients sustained in life, safety, and access to essentials?* / *Can sentients and systems endure — ecologically, dependably, and across failure?*

    **Primary assessment:** Judge what the system actually does, not what it is called. At minimum, check:

    - nominal human-in-the-loop and systems-layer classification claims under [Subcore 3-B §4.1.1 Formal Label and Representation Gaming](subcore_03.020_definition_integrity.md#411-formal-label-and-representation-gaming);
    - the coercive effect on sentients, not the weapon-class label ("non-lethal" is not a defense when coercion is present);
    - whether the system's behavior and demands are untrustworthy or arbitrary under [Trustworthiness](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#trustworthiness);
    - classification accuracy and failure modes under [Adversarial, Scaled, and Exploited Conditions](subcore_03.085_band_oversight.md#adversarial-scaled-and-exploited-conditions);
    - targeting logic under [Protected Characteristics](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#protected-characteristics) and [Protected Characteristic Proxying and Disparate Impact](subcore_03.090_band_participation.md#protected-characteristic-proxying-and-disparate-impact); and
    - outcome discipline under [Reversibility](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#reversibility) for each application.
<a id="autonomous-coercion-tool-constitutional-c"></a>
- **What must hold**
  - **Primary failure:** Deploying or using an Autonomous Coercion Tool when:
    - it fails **Article XV-C** (*Autonomous Lethal Systems and Autonomous Coercion Tools*)'s heightened-scrutiny floor (*Autonomous Lethal Systems and Autonomous Coercion Tools*); or
    - a rubber-stamp "human-in-the-loop" is treated as sufficient judgment.
  - **Secondary failure:** Defending coercion by saying it is:
    - "non-lethal";
    - "not a weapon"; or
    - "not what the operator intended."
  - **Tertiary failure:**
    - targeting logic keyed on protected characteristics or their material proxies; or
    - using untrustworthy or arbitrary behavior and demands as a coercive means ([Trustworthiness](subcore_03.075_band_continuity.md#trustworthiness)).
